# Design log

This log records consequential implementation decisions, their evidence, and the tradeoffs that future scanner work must preserve.

## 2026-08-06 — Make real X11 smoke shutdown explicit and testable

**Observed failure.** Hosted Linux GUI run [`31134089022`](https://github.com/secwest/copilot-security/actions/runs/31134089022) passed scanner packaging, locked restore, build, 7/7 core tests, 3/3 shared tests, 2/2 Linux headless tests, self-contained publish, and non-graphical startup. Its only failure was `timeout 20s xvfb-run ... --ui-smoke-test`: the real Avalonia window opened, but the one-second `DispatcherTimer` close path did not terminate the classic desktop lifetime, so the watchdog returned 124 and Xvfb teardown produced a secondary X I/O error. The equivalent local WSL probe had passed, identifying timing/lifetime nondeterminism rather than a scanner or archive defect.

**Decision.** Keep the external 20-second deadline unchanged. In UI-smoke mode, select `ShutdownMode.OnExplicitShutdown`, attach to the real window `Opened` event, post the close operation to the UI dispatcher, and call lifetime `Shutdown(0)` in a `finally` block. Closing still runs `MainWindow.OnClosed` and disposes its view model; explicit shutdown no longer relies on platform-specific inference from the last window. Expose only the small close/shutdown scheduler needed by the Linux test project.

**Regression evidence.** The existing headless surface test now configures the production UI-smoke scheduler, opens a second real headless window, drains dispatcher work, and requires both the shutdown callback and a non-visible window. Locked WSL restore and warning-free build pass, both Linux tests pass, self-contained publish succeeds, and the published binary completes five consecutive X11/Xvfb UI-smoke launches under the unchanged timeout. This focused repair is pushed separately so hosted Linux can re-run it without conflating the result with scanner-model changes.

**Hosted confirmation.** Repair checkpoint `193571271d1bd595256514e9dcbaa2cb04b33707` passes [`linux-gui-ci` run `31135258027`](https://github.com/secwest/copilot-security/actions/runs/31135258027). The formerly failing real X11 startup step passes under the unchanged watchdog, archive assembly and retention complete, and the full job succeeds in 2 minutes 23 seconds.

## 2026-08-06 — Add merge-deep through exact package and version proof

**Decision.** Add the direct callable exported by `merge-deep` to `node-http-prototype-merge`, but preserve a third independent package identity and version line. Accept default ESM imports and direct CommonJS assignments only when the matching nearest runtime dependency resolves below 3.0.3 and remote data reaches a source operand. Emit `vulnerable-merge-deep-recursive-merge` or `lock-resolved-vulnerable-merge-deep-recursive-merge` so exact metadata and lock resolution remain visible. Reject the patched boundary, wrong-package declarations, namespace/named import guesses, reassignment, and target-only data.

**Primary evidence.** CodeQL's high-precision prototype-polluting merge query treats unsafe recursive merge/extend calls as a distinct path problem and explicitly cites multiple library families beyond Lodash ([CodeQL query help](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-prototype-pollution/)). GitHub's reviewed advisory GHSA-r6rj-9ch6-g264 classifies `merge-deep` before 3.0.3 as critical, and GitHub Security Lab demonstrates the exact CommonJS callable plus `constructor.prototype` payload against 3.0.2 ([GHSL-2020-160](https://securitylab.github.com/advisories/GHSL-2020-160-merge-deep/)). This evidence supports a precise package/API/version model; it does not justify matching arbitrary `mergeDeep` method names or transferring the range to Lodash, jQuery, `extend`, or another package.

**Benchmark and correction.** Add topology-matched four-file Express fixtures pinned to 3.0.2 and 3.0.3, local historical/patched witnesses, perfect-gate specialized cases, three-run main cases, and repository-cap assertions. The first integrated run found one expected row with nine propagators but rejected it because newly chosen wrapper symbols differed from the established prototype-merge topology. Correct the fixtures to use the exact `routeOptions` / `configureOptions` / `patch` chain rather than relaxing the full propagator assertion. The accepted row retains source `src/server.js:8`, sink `src/storage.js:4`, CWE-1321, all nine ordered propagators, and the `merge-deep` sink identity; the patched twin is absent. Direct adversarial tests cover exact default/CommonJS and optional dependency proof, range plus npm lock resolution, patched exact/resolved boundaries, package-identity mismatch, namespace/named imports, and reassignment. The canonical corpus now contains 55 pairs and 330 scans. Formatting, generated output, TypeScript, both witnesses, and the focused model/corpus gate pass with 24 tests, one intentional Windows skip, no failures, and 1,223 assertions.

**Final acceptance.** Exact implementation checkpoint `8680a3b88bb51ebb2ebf7629638ae1b13fab9024` passes the authoritative suite with 1,193 tests, 9,056 assertions, 20 intentional skips, and no failures across 128 files in 277.50 seconds. Formatting, generated output, TypeScript, a clean build, and the production advisory audit are green. Two compiled inventories take 13,120 and 11,851 ms, retain 96 byte-identical rows and 253,758 bytes, and have SHA-256 `d1796ef728e7ef45355b8daee6c5217a4d0e61c06e099ec07368ee64f493cd64`. The cap simultaneously retains one core exact, core lock-resolved, standalone `lodash.merge`, and `merge-deep` row, each with the exact source/sink lines, CWE-1321, and nine propagators; every patched fixture is absent. A separate compiled WSL probe rejects a symlinked vulnerable `merge-deep` lockfile.

**Release and desktop evidence.** Strict package inspection accepts 251 entries. Three fresh Windows consumers install 80 packages each, and two Ubuntu Node 22.23.1 consumers install 88 platform-resolved packages each; all validate the public import, CLI, and 79-file bundled-plugin contract. The deliberately removed 1,576,120-byte archive had SHA-1 `8bb4a8939d68ae0057dbc7f26360ec4a7f3fd18b` and SHA-256 `abfa4e35021750e44278b82a77ac0366d8e1b4f4ebfa854dc2cfeabb1dc93e0b`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes 7/7 core and 3/3 shared tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `33d1657c6775bc00ebedc9138a336aa8e4f25ce4a5adadc19df645bfd26df916`. WSL locked restore and build are warning/error-free; 7/7 core, 3/3 shared, and 2/2 Linux tests pass, as do non-graphical and real X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

## 2026-08-06 — Model standalone lodash.merge as a separate package boundary

**Decision.** Extend the existing recursive-merge framework model to the direct callable exported by the standalone `lodash.merge` npm package, but do not treat it as an alias for core `lodash`. Accept only a default ESM import or direct CommonJS assignment, remote flow into a source operand, and matching nearest runtime dependency evidence that resolves below 4.6.2. Use package-specific exact-manifest and npm-lockfile sink identities. Reject 4.6.2 and later, namespace or named imports that do not match the package's callable export shape, reassignment, target-only data, and any case where only core `lodash` is declared. Apply the inverse isolation rule to core imports.

**Evidence and range.** The standalone advisory reports `lodash.merge` versions before 4.6.2 as vulnerable to prototype pollution and 4.6.2 as the fix ([Snyk advisory SNYK-JS-LODASHMERGE-173733](https://security.snyk.io/vuln/SNYK-JS-LODASHMERGE-173733)). That number line is independent of core Lodash's exact `_.merge` fix at 4.17.11. Reusing the core threshold or allowing either dependency declaration to authorize both import names would create both false positives and misleading remediation. Existing bounded manifest and lockfile handling already supports the dotted package name and exact installed path `packages["node_modules/lodash.merge"]`; no new metadata trust or budget is introduced.

**API and provenance boundary.** Keep model ID `node-http-prototype-merge` and CWE-1321 because the source-to-recursive-merge security contract is the same, but carry `vulnerable-lodash-merge-package-recursive-merge` or `lock-resolved-vulnerable-lodash-merge-package-recursive-merge` at the sink. This makes the package and evidence source reviewable and gives capped inventories a stable diversity category. Reviewer instructions explicitly require the matching package declaration, corresponding lock installed path, package-specific patched boundary, concrete dangerous-key recursion, and security impact; other merge packages still require their own API and advisory proof.

**Benchmark and regression evidence.** Add a four-file Express 4.6.1 exploit and topology-identical 4.6.2 control with dependency-free historical/patched witnesses. Both retain source `src/server.js:8`, sink `src/storage.js:4`, and all nine ordered relative-import/call/parameter propagators; only the vulnerable row may survive the repository cap. Direct regressions cover default and CommonJS exact pins, optional dependency proof, range plus npm lock resolution, the patched exact and resolved boundaries, package-identity mismatch, namespace/named imports, and reassignment. The canonical corpus now contains 54 pairs and 324 scans. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript, both witnesses, and the focused framework/corpus slice pass with 23 tests, one intentional Windows skip, 0 failures, and 1,201 assertions. Full exact-head package, desktop, WSL, audit, and suite evidence is recorded separately after those acceptance gates complete.

**Final acceptance.** Exact implementation checkpoint `f0bc9c1d2d9dd52a2a7b4551089c6f7b3e69856e` passes the authoritative suite with 1,192 tests, 9,034 assertions, 20 intentional skips, and no failures across 128 files in 269.00 seconds. Formatting, generated output, TypeScript, a clean build, and the production advisory audit are green. Two compiled inventories take 13,252 and 11,288 ms, retain 96 byte-identical rows and 251,959 bytes, and have SHA-256 `5a29b51f5c5004b8609e9f6fa773df3c52d0045214bc8a532c1eac9cb702e850`. Exactly one standalone-package fixture survives with source `server.js:8`, sink `storage.js:4`, CWE-1321, and nine propagators; the patched twin is absent. A separate compiled WSL probe rejects symlinked lock evidence.

**Release and desktop evidence.** Strict package inspection accepts 251 entries. Four fresh Windows consumers install 80 packages each, and two Ubuntu Node 22.23.1 consumers install 88 platform-resolved packages each; all validate the public import, CLI, and 79-file bundled-plugin contract. The deliberately removed 1,575,633-byte archive had SHA-1 `c047c2f032478eec29d5b1c093ea61cd31e2f235` and SHA-256 `a82abf6f891c344d2613b192b6f3c9dd5e823379489c5301ba296ba02da41300`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes 7/7 core and 3/3 shared tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `caa5326a295a087e26c95ff15d7370d545399cf4308732ed0320174f29c04d8c`. WSL locked restore and build are warning/error-free; 7/7 core, 3/3 shared, and 2/2 Linux tests pass, as do non-graphical and real X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Hosted evidence.** All seven workflows triggered by implementation checkpoint `f0bc9c1d2d9dd52a2a7b4551089c6f7b3e69856e` pass: Node, Windows GUI, Linux GUI, container, .NET fixtures, Go fixtures, and Java fixtures. GitHub reports the repository as public.

## 2026-08-06 — Correct core Lodash merge cutoff at 4.17.11

**Rejected assumption.** Reject the earlier use of core Lodash versions below 4.17.12 as the exact `_.merge` vulnerability range. Lodash's own 4.17.11 changelog says that release ensured `Object.prototype` is not augmented by `_.merge` ([Lodash changelog](https://github.com/lodash/lodash/wiki/Changelog)). CodeQL's current high-precision query help likewise says the merge example is vulnerable prior to 4.17.11, while showing 4.17.12 only as the recommended dependency update ([CodeQL prototype-polluting merge query](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-prototype-pollution/)). The broader `<4.17.12` customization boundary includes other Lodash prototype-pollution surfaces and cannot be transferred to this exact merge call without a false positive.

**Correction.** Change numeric comparison to admit core Lodash only before 4.17.11. Move both exact-pin and npm-lockfile positive fixtures from 4.17.11 to 4.17.10. Add exact and lock-resolved 4.17.11 as boundary negatives while retaining 4.17.21 controls. Update reviewer guidance to state that 4.17.11 and later are patched for this primitive. The dependency-free witness continues to model historical recursive traversal; it is not evidence about a version by itself, so version identity remains separately anchored in package metadata and upstream documentation.

**Evidence and consequence.** The focused model and complete corpus contract pass 22 tests and 1,179 assertions with one intentional Windows skip for the POSIX symlink case. TypeScript and generated-model drift checks pass. Whole-repository selection still retains both vulnerable 4.17.10 fixtures and excludes both patched controls. Corrected checkpoint `3f74f5ad3a124442c79d721e83af485a97cc0592` is pushed. The extensive package, GUI, WSL, and orchestration evidence at the preceding accepted checkpoint remains useful, but its claim that 4.17.11 was vulnerable is superseded. Future package expansion must keep package-specific ranges separate: the modular `lodash.merge` package is reported vulnerable below 4.6.2 and needs its own package identity and tests rather than reuse of either core-Lodash number line.

**Corrected exact-head acceptance.** The authoritative suite passes 1,191 tests and 9,012 assertions across 128 files with 20 intentional skips and no failures in 280.97 seconds. Two clean compiled inventories take 14,883 and 12,198 ms, retain 96 byte-identical rows and 248,883 bytes, and have SHA-256 `a14586585da6c04971f8f2721c1d75d2f9fd19ba3d83042fff11ee4021207b03`. Both vulnerable 4.17.10 fixtures remain with distinct exact/lock sink provenance and nine propagators; the exact and lock-resolved 4.17.11 boundaries and both 4.17.21 controls are absent. Formatting, generated output, TypeScript, and clean build are green.

## 2026-08-06 — Resolve npm ranges only through fresh lockfile evidence

**Decision.** Extend the core-Lodash recursive-merge model to a non-exact runtime declaration only when an adjacent npm lockfile proves the installed version. Accept npm `package-lock.json` or higher-precedence `npm-shrinkwrap.json` at lockfile version 2 or 3, require `packages[""]` to repeat the manifest declaration exactly, and require `packages["node_modules/lodash"].version` to be an exact numeric release below 4.17.12. Preserve the existing exact-manifest path without requiring a lock. Give lock-resolved findings the distinct `lock-resolved-vulnerable-lodash-recursive-merge` sink identity while retaining model ID `node-http-prototype-merge` and CWE-1321.

**Why this increment precedes more merge packages.** Real npm manifests commonly declare ranges, so exact-manifest-only proof discarded high-confidence installed-version evidence already present in a reproducible dependency tree. npm documents `package-lock.json` as describing the exact generated tree; lockfile versions 2 and 3 expose that tree in the `packages` map, while `npm-shrinkwrap.json` takes publication and precedence over `package-lock.json` ([npm package-lock documentation](https://docs.npmjs.com/cli/v11/configuring-npm/package-lock-json/)). CodeQL's high-precision prototype-pollution guidance likewise couples known merge semantics to vulnerable library/version evidence rather than treating a method name as proof ([CodeQL prototype pollution query help](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-prototype-pollution/)). Using the exact installed version materially improves recall on conventional projects without speculatively evaluating semver or broadening API identity. Adding `lodash.merge`, `merge-deep`, `extend`, or other packages remains valuable, but each needs separate binding, call-position, and advisory-range evidence after this common proof gap is closed.

**Freshness and registry boundary.** The manifest and lock root declarations must be identical strings. This deliberately rejects a stale lock even if its installed entry looks vulnerable. The declaration may use numeric npm registry semver syntax such as caret, tilde, comparator, hyphen, wildcard, or OR forms; aliases, tags, workspaces, filesystem paths, URLs, Git sources, prerelease text, and arbitrary package-manager syntax contain characters outside the accepted registry grammar and fail closed. Only `dependencies` and `optionalDependencies` qualify in both manifest and lock root. Lockfile v1 lacks the required `packages` proof and is rejected. Missing root package metadata, conflicting runtime declarations, a missing `node_modules/lodash` entry, or a nonnumeric installed version is rejection, not a version guess. Overrides and nested/workspace resolution remain unsupported until their exact installed-path semantics are modeled.

**Bounded metadata and package boundaries.** Keep JSON metadata outside the 2,000 executable-source slots. Preserve the existing 512-manifest/2 MiB total/256 KiB per-manifest limits and add at most 128 preferred npm locks, 4 MiB per lock and 16 MiB total. Every read remains repository-contained, canonical, regular-file-only, symlink-free, and NUL-free. If shrinkwrap exists but is malformed, unsafe, or over budget, do not fall back to package-lock because npm itself gives shrinkwrap precedence. Likewise, the presence of an unreadable, oversized, symlinked, or otherwise rejected nearest `package.json` now stops ancestor lookup; inheriting a parent version across a real child package boundary could create a false vulnerable-version proof.

**Cap-level provenance correction.** The first whole-repository result was rejected even though both fixtures passed alone: at the unchanged 96-row limit, category diversity retained the lexically earlier lock-resolved exploit and pruned the prior exact-pin exploit because both shared one sink category. Increasing the prompt budget would hide rather than model the distinction. Exact declarations and lock-resolved declarations now retain separate sink kinds, which is truthful evidence provenance and gives the selector a stable category for each. The corrected regression requires both vulnerable fixtures and excludes both patched twins under the same cap. This provenance must survive future serialization and prompt changes.

**Benchmark and current evidence.** Add a four-file Express range/lock exploit resolving 4.17.11 and a topology-identical control resolving 4.17.21. Both use `lodash: ^4.17.0`, preserve source `src/server.js:8`, sink `src/storage.js:4`, and all nine relative-import/call/parameter propagators. Their witnesses model vulnerable and patched recursion locally without installing Lodash. The perfect-gate manifest now has two exploit/control pairs; the canonical three-run manifest has 53 pairs and 318 scans. The previous prose count was stale and is corrected to the executable manifest and pairing assertion. Direct tests cover v2/v3 caret, comparator, wildcard, shrinkwrap precedence, stale declaration, invalid higher-precedence shrinkwrap, v1, patched resolution, missing installed entry, alias/workspace/tag rejection, malformed and oversized locks, oversized child manifests, and POSIX lock symlinks. Windows passes 6 tests and 32 assertions with the POSIX-only case skipped; the adjacent prototype/corpus slice passes 27 tests and 1,200 assertions. Formatting, generated output, and TypeScript checks are clean at pushed implementation checkpoint `3e34ac540ce782221b613d585e63ba2321e7cd3f`. Full package, GUI, Linux, audit, and compiled self-review acceptance is recorded only after the exact documentation head completes those gates.

**Final acceptance.** Exact accepted checkpoint `1bb704b8a203ed66f142ac544a6e4abfd63bd1ef` passes formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript, a clean build, and a production audit with no known vulnerabilities. The authoritative serial Windows run passes 1,191 tests and 9,012 assertions across 128 files with 20 intentional skips and no failures in 300.36 seconds. WSL Node 22.23.1 independently exercises the Windows-skipped POSIX symlink boundary and rejects a valid vulnerable-version lock reached only through a symbolic link. Two compiled repository inventories take 13,455 and 11,993 ms, each contain 96 rows and 248,691 bytes, and are byte-identical at SHA-256 `37fad0b7dfd11aeb385ba26a2f11035f7443ae80e4ed9bcbb923319acabd793f`. Both vulnerable Lodash rows survive with distinct sink proof, exact source/sink lines, CWE-1321, and nine propagators; neither patched twin survives.

**Release and desktop evidence.** Strict package inspection accepts 251 entries. Three fresh Windows installations each install 80 packages; Ubuntu installs 88 platform-resolved packages. All four validate the public import, installed CLI, and 79-file bundled-plugin contract. The deliberately removed 1,574,935-byte archive had SHA-1 `f6cb66e9b2b008f0aef00e549d2117e987ce9272` and SHA-256 `ca3f334f40726d46b93305486ff4b2a373cf2cf68d7cf005e5a7fcf025334a3f`. Windows GUI build is warning/error-free, passes 7/7 core and 3/3 shared tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `59bfac3e4fed206d2d7ef65d18e10206d2314d88fb5c957060e65d0844e2bde8`. WSL locked restore and build are warning/error-free; 7/7 core, 3/3 shared, and 2/2 Linux interface tests pass, as do non-graphical and real X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Acceptance boundaries.** The first strict package attempt was rejected when the managed sandbox denied npm permission to create its normal cache temp directory. Windows publication likewise first failed because that sandbox could not reach NuGet's vulnerability service and the project promotes NU1900 to an error. Unchanged reruns at the normal package/network boundary pass; no source, dependency, or warning policy was weakened. The repository API confirms public visibility. No hosted workflow exists for the exact accepted commit because [GitHub's current incident](https://www.githubstatus.com/incidents/qcvjkzcs7j74) reports Actions as a major outage and says push and pull-request webhook triggers remain throttled, so many events are not creating runs. This is recorded as external availability rather than a passing or failing product signal.

## 2026-08-06 — Replace vulnerable PDF.js despite existing parser hardening

**Decision.** Upgrade the exact `pdfjs-dist` runtime dependency from 5.6.205 to 6.2.108 immediately after the production audit identified GHSA-hq66-cqwq-w95j / CVE-2026-16633. Keep the existing PDF knowledge-base worker limits and `isEvalSupported: false`; dependency remediation supplements rather than replaces defense in depth. Refresh the lockfile-generated optional `@napi-rs/canvas` graph and require the complete document parser regression plus package checks before acceptance.

**Why.** The GitHub-reviewed advisory rates the issue high severity (CVSS 8.6), affects versions from 5.6.83 before 6.2.108, and states that opening a malicious PDF can execute unrestricted attacker-controlled JavaScript in the hosting domain when scripting is enabled and CSP does not block it ([GitHub advisory](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-hq66-cqwq-w95j)). Disabling scripting or enforcing CSP is a workaround, not a reason to retain a vulnerable parser. The scanner's use is server-side text extraction in a resource-limited worker rather than a browser viewer, and `isEvalSupported: false` narrows exposure, but untrusted knowledge-base PDFs remain an attacker-controlled parser boundary. A production scanner should remove the affected dependency range even when configuration likely blocks the published exploit path.

**Compatibility and evidence.** PDF.js 6.2.108 requires Node 22.13 or later, which remains inside the package's already tested Node 22/24/26 contract and the pinned WSL Node 22.23.1 acceptance runtime. The upgrade removes `node-readable-to-web-readable-stream`, moves optional canvas from 0.1.100 to 1.0.3, and changes no scanner API. Supply-chain policy accepted the refreshed lockfile; `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level high` reports no known vulnerabilities; generated-model checking, TypeScript, and a clean build pass; and all six active PDF/DOCX knowledge-base tests pass with two Windows platform skips. A first test under the managed execution sandbox failed with `EPERM` while Bun loaded the newly downloaded PDF.js module. Repeating under the normal elevated test boundary passed without source changes, proving an execution-sandbox restriction rather than an API or parser regression. The pre-upgrade 1,571,967-byte npm archive is rejected and must be deleted; only a rebuilt post-upgrade archive may be accepted.

**Final acceptance.** At exact pushed dependency checkpoint `9cd2d09837f48c843c9adc4564d90e96f3422e6a`, the authoritative serial suite passes 1,190 tests and 8,990 assertions across 128 files with 19 intentional skips and no failures in 306.61 seconds. Formatting, generated output, TypeScript, clean build, document parsing, and production audit are green. The rebuilt post-upgrade archive contains 251 entries and passes three fresh Windows installations plus Ubuntu Node 22.23.1; each validates public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 plugin files. Its 1,571,972 bytes had SHA-1 `bf2113cbb66e22d456ddf102461e77781f2ffa7b` and SHA-256 `ddc7442ad91e6a280e69d189088863a73913e373df682360a7dbcc0f677ed3a0` before deliberate deletion. Windows and WSL GUI acceptance also pass. Exact-head hosted runs are absent because [GitHub's official status](https://www.githubstatus.com/incidents/qcvjkzcs7j74) continues to report an Actions major outage and throttled push/pull-request webhook triggers; this is an external availability boundary, not a suppressed failure.

## 2026-08-06 — Require exact runtime version proof for recursive Lodash merge

**Decision.** Add `node-http-prototype-merge` as a separate Node/TypeScript model for remote object flow into source operands of an official Lodash recursive merge call. Activate a row only when the nearest package boundary declares runtime package `lodash` at an exact numeric version below 4.17.12. Preserve source, call position, all relative-module wrappers, `vulnerable-lodash-recursive-merge` sink identity, and CWE-1321. A patched exact version emits no specialized row. Do not reuse shallow `Object.assign`, nested computed-write, or generic method-name semantics.

**Why.** CodeQL's high-precision prototype-polluting merge query requires both a known recursive merge API and a vulnerable dependency version; its current JavaScript customization identifies core Lodash releases before 4.17.12 as vulnerable ([CodeQL prototype-polluting merge call](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-prototype-pollution/), [CodeQL prototype-pollution customizations](https://github.com/github/codeql/blob/main/javascript/ql/lib/semmle/javascript/security/dataflow/PrototypePollutionCustomizations.qll)). The earlier scanner could model request-controlled nested keys and shallow built-in copy but intentionally left library identity, recursion, and version unresolved. A loose `.merge(...)` match would confuse application helpers, unrelated packages, patched releases, targets with sources, and shallow copy. Exact dependency and operand proof reduces those false positives while recovering a real multi-file class.

**Bounded package evidence.** Keep generic JSON outside `SOURCE_EXTENSIONS` and the 2,000-file source cap. After source discovery, locate only the nearest `package.json` for each JavaScript/TypeScript file, stop at the first package boundary even when it is malformed, and retain unique manifests solely as metadata. Bound this auxiliary read to 512 manifests, 2 MiB total, the existing 256 KiB per-file limit, repository containment, regular files, no symlinks, and no NUL bytes. This avoids displacing executable source in large monorepositories and prevents dependency proof from becoming an unbounded secondary crawl. Parse only `dependencies` and `optionalDependencies`; reject absent, non-string, conflicting, ranged, tagged, aliased, workspace, file, Git, prerelease, peer-only, and development-only declarations. Lockfile-resolved ranges are a later contract, not an inference.

**Binding, version, and position proof.** Accept a default or namespace import from `lodash`, a whole-package CommonJS receiver, a named/destructured `merge` binding, or a direct default/CommonJS binding from `lodash/merge` or `lodash/merge.js`. Require the binding before the call, reject wrapper-parameter shadowing and identifier reassignment through the sink line, and reject receiver-member reassignment through the sink line. Parse bounded multiline arguments and trace only operands one and later; request data confined to the destination object is outside this API contract. The pinned version parser accepts only `major.minor.patch` and performs numeric comparison with 4.17.12. This first lane deliberately covers core `lodash`, not the separately versioned `lodash.merge` package.

**Impact and reviewer obligations.** A vulnerable version and remote source make the call reviewable, not automatically exploitable. Require proof that the concrete merge implementation recursively follows `__proto__` or `constructor.prototype`, reaches `Object.prototype` or another security-relevant prototype, and causes inherited authorization state, code or template execution, denial of service, or another concrete effect. Independently inspect request parsing, own versus inherited keys, null-prototype destinations, schema rejection, dangerous-key guards, lockfile resolution, and all merge sources. Patched dependencies, object spread, shallow copy, jQuery/extend families, application-defined merge helpers, and arbitrary dependency ranges need separate semantic and version contracts.

**Executable and regression evidence.** The positive fixture preserves Express `request.body` at `src/server.js:8`, nine ordered import/call/parameter propagators, and `lodash.merge()` at `src/storage.js:4` under a 4.17.11 runtime pin. Its topology-identical control pins 4.17.21 and emits no specialized row. Dependency-free witnesses reproduce the historical `constructor.prototype` traversal and a patched dangerous-key boundary without installing a vulnerable third-party package. Direct regressions cover default, namespace, CommonJS, destructured, subpath, and optional-runtime forms plus patched, ranged, dev-only, target-only, lookalike, reassigned, same-line mutation, missing-manifest, malformed-manifest, and literal negatives. The focused model passes 5 tests and 21 assertions; the adjacent prototype and complete corpus lanes pass 31 tests and 1,201 assertions. Formatting, generated-model drift, and TypeScript checking pass.

**Whole-product acceptance.** Two compiled exact-head self-reviews take 12,255 and 12,487 ms and produce 96 byte-identical rows, 245,933 bytes, and SHA-256 `2f0acf30b3855e29176cea1aa65d6cd187a559f2b527e507fba28dd6e16dc968`. The sole `node-http-prototype-merge` row retains the exact Express source, storage sink, CWE-1321, and nine propagators; the patched fixture remains absent. The authoritative suite and package evidence are recorded in the preceding PDF.js section because the audit-driven dependency remediation became part of the same accepted exact head. Windows publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `de2e03feda4ff3437e6aa0bc4be275c8df9683c4b74c3deea85782c3c51509ec` after zero-warning/error build, 7/7 core tests, and 3/3 shared tests. WSL performs locked restore, zero-warning/error build, 7/7 core, 3/3 shared, and 2/2 Linux tests plus non-graphical and real X11/Xvfb startup; its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Corpus and failure boundary.** Add one exploit/control pair to the main three-run corpus and a perfect-gate single-run manifest requiring exact location, CWE-1321, severity, validation, attack path, code evidence, stability, negative-control behavior, and zero false positives. The versioned corpus now describes 81 pairs and 516 main scans. The whole-repository regression proves the vulnerable row survives the unchanged 96-row cap while the patched twin remains absent. Unsupported surfaces include semver ranges resolved by lockfiles, npm aliases and overrides, Yarn Plug'n'Play, pnpm catalog/workspace indirection, `lodash.merge` package releases, other CodeQL-listed merge libraries, dynamic imports, arbitrary binding aliases, calls beyond three relative imports, and application-defined recursive utilities. Each expansion needs an exact package/call/version contract and paired executable evidence.

## 2026-08-06 — Separate shallow `Object.assign()` prototype replacement from recursive merge

**Decision.** Add `node-http-prototype-copy` as an exact Node/TypeScript model for remote object flow into one or more source arguments of the built-in `Object.assign()`. Preserve the target expression separately, retain source-position flow through the existing three-boundary relative-module graph, and classify a proven security-relevant prototype change as CWE-1321. An exact `Object.create(null)` target remains visible as strong setter-specific counterevidence. Do not fold object spread, recursive merge/extend utilities, or remote data used only as the assignment target into this contract.

**Why.** CodeQL ships a high-precision prototype-polluting merge query because unsafe recursive merge utilities can traverse attacker keys such as `constructor.prototype` and modify `Object.prototype` ([CodeQL prototype-polluting merge call](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-prototype-pollution/)). The built-in shallow copy has narrower semantics. `Object.assign()` reads enumerable own source properties and performs `[[Set]]` on the target, invoking inherited setters ([MDN `Object.assign`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Object/assign)). An own JSON `__proto__` property can therefore replace an ordinary target's prototype, but shallow assignment alone usually does not modify global `Object.prototype`; object spread defines data properties and does not invoke the setter ([MDN prototype pollution](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Security/Attacks/Prototype_pollution)). Treating these three forms as interchangeable would inflate severity, invent global impact, and misclassify a real safe alternative.

**Receiver and position proof.** Activate only exact `Object.assign(...)` syntax after comment/string masking, parse bounded multiline arguments, require at least one target and one nonempty source, and resolve taint only against arguments one and later. Remote data confined to argument zero is not copied into a security target by this API contract. Reject a wrapper parameter named `Object`, file-level variable/function/class/import/catch bindings named `Object`, direct reassignment of `Object`, and assignment or mutation of `Object.assign` before the call. This is deliberately conservative global-identity proof; dynamic mutation through `Object.defineProperty`, realm replacement, proxies, or aliases remains outside the exact lane.

**Impact and counterevidence.** The reviewer must prove that the source parser produced an enumerable own `__proto__` property, the target inherited the legacy setter, the assigned value became the target's prototype, and a subsequent inherited lookup caused concrete authorization, code/template execution, denial-of-service, or other adverse behavior. A null-prototype target has no inherited setter, so the hostile name becomes ordinary own data; this closes setter-based prototype replacement but does not authorize mass assignment of other own fields. Schema validation with additional properties rejected or a complete allowlist can be strong only when it dominates every source before the call. Up-to-date library versions, recursion behavior, and `constructor.prototype` reachability belong to the later recursive-merge contract rather than being inferred here.

**Red/green and executable evidence.** The strict red baseline passed only manifest shape and failed the remaining 3 tests with 11 assertions: neither real fixture nor direct call emitted the new row, and the reviewer lacked source-position and shallow-copy semantics. The implemented model passes 5 tests and 23 assertions. The vulnerable four-file fixture preserves the Express source at `src/server.js:8`, nine ordered propagators, and the exact `Object.assign` call at `src/storage.js:4` with no control. Its topology-matched twin retains the same row plus only `null-prototype-assignment-target`. Direct regressions accept direct, aliased, and later source arguments and reject target-only flow, local or parameter shadowing, member reassignment, lookalikes, spread, and strings. The vulnerable witness proves one options target inherits attacker authorization state while a fresh ordinary object does not; the control proves a null prototype, an own retained `__proto__` data property, no inherited authorization state, and preserved fixed defaults.

**Whole-repository prioritization correction.** The first 96-row cap result was rejected: the safe fixture sorted first by path and its unique candidate-control category seeded it, while the otherwise identical unmitigated row was pruned. Reuse the deliberately narrow established-boundary ordering rule and add only `null-prototype-assignment-target` on `node-http-prototype-copy`; the ordinary target now ranks first and category diversity still retains the controlled twin. Do not generalize this into sorting all rows by control count because candidate controls include incomplete review evidence and the previous broad experiment reordered unrelated GORM, pgconn, and Java path contracts. The corrected copy, nested-key, corpus, Mongoose update, bulk, and aggregation lanes pass 47 tests and 1,323 assertions, including every historical prioritization assertion.

**Acceptance evidence.** The authoritative suite passes 1,185 tests and 8,958 assertions across 127 files with 19 intentional environment/platform skips and no failures in 276.49 seconds. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass. Two compiled self-reviews take 12,193 and 11,661 ms and produce 96 byte-identical rows, 246,323 bytes, and SHA-256 `b077f1cc5045b160ea6fd496463bb35bcfd8fb769bf2a3fc132894a1999b0a09`; both shallow-copy rows survive with exact endpoints and nine propagators, in unmitigated-then-controlled order. Strict archive inspection validates 251 entries. Three independent Windows installations and one Ubuntu installation under supported Node 22.23.1 validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,564,635-byte archive had SHA-1 `e46ad99b0300e44ec2b72da9cb5b284b5964f862` and SHA-256 `7aa2d8ccbdae406deea3ae054783b16451e3263b86bf9ace65f68f0c1e30d7f9`.

**Desktop and hosted evidence.** Windows builds with zero warnings or errors, passes seven scanner-core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `797859fbc18e67a56e2dcf895f7e15f2c38f175d2db5b7d51f38d244cfe13267`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores under a Linux-only path, builds with zero warnings or errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes non-graphical plus real X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable retains SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`. Exact pushed code checkpoint `b6e1b8f9868fa66e2ab2eff1b101283184721aec` has no hosted workflow runs. [GitHub's official incident](https://www.githubstatus.com/incidents/qcvjkzcs7j74) still classifies Actions as a major outage and reports that only a minority of push and pull-request webhooks are triggering workflow runs, so hosted absence is an external availability boundary rather than a scanner failure.

**Corpus and failure boundary.** Add the pair to the main three-run corpus and a perfect-gate specialized manifest requiring exact location, CWE-1321, severity, validation, attack path, code evidence, and zero false positives. The versioned corpus now describes eighty exploit/control pairs and 510 main scans. Arbitrary aliases of `Object.assign`, destructured built-ins, cross-realm objects, proxies, target factories hidden behind helpers, `Object.defineProperty` mutation, `for...in` copy loops, jQuery/extend APIs, lodash and other recursive merge versions, application-defined recursive utilities, and flows beyond three relative imports remain residual-review surfaces. Each needs its own receiver/version/recursion and exploit/control contract rather than a broader method-name match.

## 2026-08-06 — Trace remote nested keys into prototype-reachable objects

**Decision.** Add `node-http-prototype-pollution` as an exact Node/TypeScript framework model for plain assignments with at least two dynamic computed property-name positions. Resolve the HTTP source from those key expressions only, then reuse the bounded relative-module summary graph to retain up to three import/call/parameter boundaries. Classify the sink as `nested-computed-property-write` with CWE-1321. Do not broaden the contract to one-level dictionaries, assigned values, merge APIs, spread, deserialization, or same-named `Map` operations.

**Why.** An ordinary object lookup through attacker-selected nested names can traverse `__proto__` to `Object.prototype`; a second selected name can then install an inherited property affecting unrelated objects. CodeQL models prototype-polluting assignment as a high-precision JavaScript query and recommends prototype-free maps, constant prefixes, or strict rejection of `__proto__`, `constructor`, and `prototype` ([CodeQL prototype-polluting assignment](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-prototype-polluting-assignment/)). Its JavaScript suite also separates prototype-polluting assignment, function, merge, and remote-property-injection queries, which argues for distinct semantic contracts rather than one loose property-write regex. Snyk likewise exposes JavaScript prototype-pollution sinks and sanitizers as a dedicated CWE-1321 rule family ([Snyk JavaScript and TypeScript rules](https://docs.snyk.io/scan-with-snyk/snyk-code/snyk-code-security-rules/javascript-and-typescript-rules)). The prior scanner had only a broad lexical dynamic-property/prototype review signal and one same-file generic corpus case, so it could not preserve this exact multi-file key-position path.

**Exact syntax and provenance.** Mask comments and literal contents before structural matching, but slice expressions from the byte-aligned original line so recorded source text remains meaningful. Examine every plain `=` assignment on the line; an earlier declaration or assignment cannot hide a later sink. Require two or more non-literal bracket expressions on the left-hand side. Resolve taint against their joined expressions, never the right-hand side. Consequently `target[request.body.namespace][request.body.key] = value` is eligible, while `target[localNamespace][localKey] = request.body.value`, `target[request.body.key] = value`, `target["fixed"][request.body.key] = value`, equality checks, and `+=` are not. This is deliberately a high-precision reachability hypothesis: the reviewer must still prove that the base object has a prototype-bearing path and that the selected names reach a security-relevant prototype.

**Validation and counterevidence.** Require a concrete `__proto__`, `constructor`, or `prototype` path and an inherited-property, authorization, code/template execution, denial-of-service, or comparable effect before reporting. Nested `Map` storage is strong key-isolation evidence because special names remain ordinary data. `Object.create(null)`, a constant prefix, a strict allowlist, or fail-closed rejection of all three dangerous names is relevant only when it dominates every attacker-controlled segment before the same write. A denylist fragment, input encoding, request authentication, or data confined to the assigned value does not establish key safety. `Object.assign`, object spread, recursive merge helpers, and JSON parsing have different trigger and propagation semantics and remain in residual review until each receives its own exact source, receiver, position, and control contract.

**Red/green and executable boundary.** The strict red baseline passed manifest shape and failed three behavior/guidance assertions because the real multi-hop fixture and direct nested write emitted no exact row and the quality gate lacked prototype-specific obligations. The implemented lane passes 5 tests and 21 assertions. The positive preserves the Express `request.body` source at `src/server.js:8`, nine ordered propagators across gateway, service, and storage, and the sink at `src/storage.js:4`; its `Map` twin emits no row. The adversarial matrix accepts a direct key-position flow and the same sink after an earlier same-line assignment, while rejecting value-only, one-key, fixed-first-key, comparison, compound-assignment, `Map`, and string cases. A whole-repository regression proves the exploit survives the unchanged 96-row cap and the control does not. The vulnerable witness writes `isAdministrator` through the `__proto__` namespace and observes it on a fresh object, then cleans up in `finally`; the control retains the same keys in nested maps without changing `Object.prototype`.

**Acceptance evidence.** The authoritative suite passes 1,180 tests and 8,923 assertions across 126 files with 19 intentional environment/platform skips and no failures in 280.29 seconds. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass. Two compiled self-reviews take 12,849 and 12,640 ms and produce 96 byte-identical rows, 242,422 bytes, and SHA-256 `a0bbe579ac49e438a69b1a03fc9615c206a9ed2e6ef0247e24b5d753c89ec34f`; the exact new row survives with nine propagators and no control, while the `Map` twin remains absent. Strict archive inspection validates 251 entries. Three independent Windows installations and one Ubuntu installation under supported Node 22.23.1 validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,561,574-byte archive had SHA-1 `c594cf58472a4a0ef27ff4e5ee8af89788198a00` and SHA-256 `28e2eb33b048feef65cd44bc65179be9fce6ccf9298f00dcc8b895eff6d7bb2c`.

**Desktop and hosted evidence.** Windows builds with zero warnings or errors, passes seven scanner-core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `4047af42434f851341bcc1b7ee6359e82ac6514dca52d9b37c45fb9ad7fc5c58`. Its first publish attempt was not counted because the managed network boundary prevented NuGet vulnerability-metadata refresh and warnings are errors; the identical host-boundary rerun passed without weakening policy. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores under a Linux-only runtime path, builds with zero warnings or errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes both non-graphical startup and a real X11/Xvfb window. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`. The exact pushed code checkpoint `59eefc780965ffcd5a91e1917232ad14af5881a2` has no hosted workflow runs. [GitHub's official incident](https://www.githubstatus.com/incidents/qcvjkzcs7j74) still classifies Actions as a major outage and reports that push and pull-request webhook triggers are heavily throttled, so exact-head hosted absence is an external availability boundary rather than a scanner failure.

**Corpus and failure boundary.** Add the pair to the main three-run corpus and to a perfect-gate single-run manifest requiring CWE-1321, exact location, validation, attack path, code evidence, severity, and zero false positives. The versioned corpus now describes seventy-nine exploit/control pairs and 504 main scans. Deliberately unsupported forms include nested bracket expressions split across lines, optional chaining, private fields, computed keys hidden behind arbitrary helper returns, writes beyond three relative-import boundaries, inherited setters other than the exact nested-key shape, and merge/copy/deserialization APIs. Future expansion must add paired witnesses and position-specific negative controls rather than weakening the two-key proof.

## 2026-08-06 — Extend exact aggregation proof through `Aggregate.append()`

**Decision.** Extend `node-http-mongoose-aggregate` with an origin-aware `Aggregate.append()` model rather than matching every method named `append` or treating mutation as dispatch. Start from the already proven official Mongoose Model and exact `Model.aggregate()` result. Parse the documented single object, single array, and multiple-argument forms; resolve bounded stage aliases; and attach each tainted position to the append call's source line. For an assigned Aggregate, require the exact receiver in the same exported wrapper, no reassignment, and a later receiver-exact execution. For a chain, require immediate ordered consumption or the established async-return boundary.

**Why.** Mongoose documents `Aggregate.prototype.append(...ops)` as accepting either multiple stage objects or one array, mutating the pipeline, returning the same Aggregate, and not itself executing the lazy operation ([Mongoose Aggregate API](https://mongoosejs.com/docs/api/aggregate.html)). MongoDB gives appended stages the same executable selection, transformation, join, expression, and collection-write semantics as stages present in the initial pipeline ([MongoDB aggregation stages](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/current/reference/operator/aggregation-pipeline/), [MongoDB `$merge`](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/current/reference/operator/aggregation/merge/)). Ignoring later mutation therefore creates a false negative, but name-only or co-occurrence matching creates false positives for application buffers, different Aggregate instances, inspection, or already-consumed pipelines.

**Ordering and identity proof.** Chained append calls are parsed only in the `Model.aggregate()` statement and only before the first chained execution method. Assigned calls are searched only for the exact declared Aggregate receiver inside the containing exported wrapper. `await aggregate.append(...)` and an async return of the same append result are consumption because append returns the same thenable Aggregate; otherwise the model requires a later `aggregate.exec()`, `then`, `catch`, `finally`, or `cursor`, `await aggregate`, or async return of `aggregate`. A generic later `.exec()` is insufficient: the regression includes a different receiver with that method and requires no finding. Append after the only execution, `pipeline()` inspection, unrelated same-named methods, and receiver reassignment fail closed.

**Classification and counterevidence.** Initial and appended positions retain separate sink kinds and exact source lines, while write-stage presence is recomputed over their combined ordered pipeline. An appended dynamic stage before fixed `$merge` or `$out` is therefore `mongoose-aggregate-appended-input-before-write-stage` with CWE-943 and CWE-915 rather than losing integrity impact; appended `$match`/`$redact` remains CWE-943, and appended write structure carries both IDs. The existing `fixed-aggregate-match-value-boundary` applies only to exact `$eq` beneath a fixed literal `$match` field and is attributed to the append line. A fixed array, outer object, projection, destination, validator, or sanitizer name is not elevated into universal pipeline safety.

**Red/green and executable evidence.** The strict red baseline passed 5 tests and failed 3 with 67 assertions: the original aggregation cases still worked, but both new fixtures produced no rows, documented append forms produced no direct records, and the reviewer prompt lacked append guidance. The strengthened lane passes 9 tests and 78 assertions plus TypeScript checking. It covers chained arrays, multiple stage arguments, assigned arrays, awaited returned appends, appended pre-write grammar, exact line and origin metadata, and pipeline-wide write impact. Negative cases cover append after the only execution, inspection-only use, unrelated append methods, and a later `exec()` on a different receiver. The vulnerable four-file witness appends attacker-selected `$lookup` and projection stages and exposes a signing key; its topology-matched control rejects operator objects through exact `$eq` and projects only public fields.

**Final local and hosted acceptance.** Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass. The four adjacent Mongoose lanes pass 26 tests and 185 assertions, and all six aggregation witnesses pass. The authoritative suite passes 1,175 tests and 8,891 assertions across 125 files with 19 intentional environment/platform skips and no failures. The ordinary `pnpm test` wrapper was not counted because the restarted Windows shell did not expose Bun on `PATH`; its pretest build passed, and the established absolute pinned Bun 1.3.14 invocation ran the complete authoritative suite. Two compiled self-reviews take 13,268 and 12,783 ms and produce 96 byte-identical rows, 242,966 bytes, and SHA-256 `7d2566b22e5c78485042c5c82df1414c142fd49457f68a5cf7f341f222b3d28b`. All six aggregate rows survive the unchanged cap with distinct initial/appended and read/write identities. Strict archive inspection initially encountered only managed-sandbox denial at npm's user-cache temporary directory; the host-boundary rerun passed all path, duplicate, metadata, internal-reference, executable, installed-import, CLI, and complete 79-file plugin-contract checks across 251 entries. Three independent Windows installs and one Ubuntu install under supported Node 22.23.1 pass. The removed 1,558,170-byte archive had SHA-1 `b7b5c9171f2e6a5e9b30272292f09a4660778c38` and SHA-256 `f6c5c2bc51c74909ac443651113a16d6e69794fe1e91f5921cdf8283a017eae0`.

**Desktop and service evidence.** Windows builds with zero warnings or errors, passes seven scanner-core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `b9d42cabd58597cfbba24decf7cad66dfff9080bf9d27eb10649d4d542549b83`. WSL Ubuntu regenerates the platform-specific restore graph in locked mode under a Linux-only path, builds with zero warnings or errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes both non-graphical startup and a real X11/Xvfb window startup. Its 72,568-byte executable retains SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`. The exact pushed code checkpoint `7c4822d17d48ec0560139d4112fcb86d0862b348` has zero hosted runs. At the same check, [GitHub's official status](https://www.githubstatus.com/) marks Actions as a major outage and its critical incident says workflow runs are failing, delayed, or timing out. Hosted absence is therefore an external availability boundary, not a scanner failure; exact-head workflow acceptance remains pending recovery.

**Failure boundary.** This increment does not infer mutations made by directly editing the array returned by `pipeline()`, plugin-defined helpers, arbitrary Mongoose re-exports, dynamically selected receivers, runtime monkey patching, or stage assembly beyond the bounded expression resolver. Those surfaces remain in residual review until they have exact receiver, ordering, execution, and paired exploit/control evidence.

## 2026-08-06 — Model Mongoose aggregation as executable read and write grammar

**Decision.** Add `node-http-mongoose-aggregate` as a separate exact host model rather than broadening selector, update, or bulk-write matching. Reuse official default, namespace, named-`model`, and CommonJS factory proof plus the unique non-reassigned Model identity. Parse only argument zero of exact `Model.aggregate()` calls, resolve bounded aliases and literal arrays, and retain the complete pipeline, dynamic or spread stages, `$match` and `$redact` expressions, `$lookup`, `$graphLookup`, and `$unionWith` cross-collection stages, and `$merge` or `$out` write stages as distinct positions. Argument one and later aggregate options are outside the pipeline model.

**Why.** Mongoose documents `Model.aggregate()` as producing an Aggregate over a pipeline array, explicitly warns that it does not cast pipeline stages, and documents `exec()`, promise consumption, and `cursor()` as execution surfaces ([Mongoose Aggregate API](https://mongoosejs.com/docs/api/aggregate.html)). MongoDB's aggregation stage catalog includes document selection and transformation, joins and collection unions, server expressions, and collection-writing `$merge` and `$out` stages, so a pipeline is executable database grammar rather than a filter-shaped value ([MongoDB aggregation stages](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/current/reference/operator/aggregation-pipeline/), [MongoDB `$merge`](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/current/reference/operator/aggregation/merge/)). CodeQL's JavaScript custom-model contract exposes `nosql-injection` as a sink kind, supporting CWE-943 for attacker-selected query or stage language. CWE-915 is additionally appropriate when a complete pipeline or write-stage structure selects unintended fields or destinations ([CodeQL custom JavaScript library models](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/customizing-library-models-for-javascript/)).

**Execution and classification.** Unlike `bulkWrite()`, `aggregate()` returns a lazy Aggregate. A candidate therefore requires `await`, return from an exact async wrapper, `exec`, `then`, `catch`, `finally`, or `cursor`; construction followed only by `pipeline()` or `model()` inspection is not dispatch. A dynamic complete pipeline can choose both query and write stages and therefore carries CWE-943 plus CWE-915. `$merge` and `$out` positions carry both IDs; match, redaction, lookup, graph-lookup, union, and generic stages carry CWE-943. The quality gate must prove collection reachability, selected documents, projection or redaction, tenant and object authorization, protected fields, server expressions and resource cost, database roles, fixed write destinations and policies, acknowledgement, and concrete confidentiality, integrity, authorization, or availability impact.

**Counterevidence.** Retain `fixed-aggregate-match-value-boundary` only when the same tainted value is wrapped by exact `$eq` beneath a fixed literal `$match` field. A raw request value is not assumed scalar: JSON can still supply an operator object. A complete object, computed field, spread, `$regex`, other operator, expression, cross-collection name, `$merge.into`, or `$out` target does not qualify. A literal outer array is not sufficient because literal stage structure can still contain request-controlled collection names, expressions, operators, or destinations. `mongoose.sanitizeFilter` applies to filters and is not a universal aggregation-pipeline sanitizer; schema casting likewise does not establish safety because Mongoose does not cast aggregation stages. The control is evidence for review, not automatic suppression.

**Red/green boundary.** Before implementation, the manifest-shape assertion passed while the four behavior and reviewer assertions failed: all four real fixtures and eight accepted direct cases emitted zero aggregate rows, no control was retained, and the quality gate lacked aggregation guidance. The strengthened boundary passes 23 adjacent Mongoose tests and 169 assertions plus TypeScript checking. Each real fixture retains nine ordered relative-import/call/parameter propagators to `src/storage.js:12`; only the exact-`$eq` twins carry counterevidence. Adversarial direct cases accept await, async return, exec, then, catch, finally, cursor, multiline chaining, assigned-await consumption, match, lookup, merge, and spread-stage execution while rejecting options-only, inert, lookalike, Model-reassigned, and Aggregate-reassigned cases. The four executable witnesses prove signing-key disclosure and role/MFA replacement, while their controls reject operator objects, project only public data, retain protected state, and keep the write destination fixed. The write exploit supplies attacker-selected mutation stages before that fixed destination, proving that destination ownership alone does not authorize fields.

**Whole-repository prioritization boundary.** The first compiled acceptance pass was byte-stable at 96 rows but rejected: category seeding retained the lexically first dynamic-pipeline exploit and fixed-match control while pruning the `$merge` exploit and safe-write twin. The host could not distinguish two complete dynamic pipeline parameters from their call sites, and the global cap correctly refused to infer runtime stages. The correction changes the write exploit to the equally dangerous and statically provable pattern of attacker-controlled stages spread before a fixed `$merge`. Every parsed position now records whether its enclosing literal pipeline contains `$merge` or `$out`; dynamic input before such a stage becomes `mongoose-aggregate-input-before-write-stage`, and a tainted filter becomes `mongoose-aggregate-filter-before-write-stage`. CWE-943 remains exact for controlled selection; dynamic pre-write grammar also retains CWE-915. This provides honest category diversity without path-specific logic or a larger prompt budget. A whole-repository regression requires all four aggregate fixture rows and their exact sink kinds under the unchanged 96-row cap. Corrected compiled passes take 12,808 and 12,258 ms, produce 238,538-byte identical inventories with SHA-256 `871320aec642aafccb503f95afdf9ef5a5bf76152b815c4742ab6b586d88ee13`, and retain the four rows in dangerous-read, dangerous-write, controlled-read, controlled-write order.

**Failure boundary.** This increment deliberately does not infer arbitrary Mongoose re-exports, discriminators, dynamic/computed Model identities, plugin-provided aggregate helpers, direct mutation through the array returned by `.pipeline()`, callback plugins, or runtime monkey patching. Assigned Aggregate consumption is bounded to the same exported wrapper and fails closed on reassignment or inspection-only use. Documented `.append()` mutation is covered by the later origin-aware extension above; other expansion still requires an API-specific execution and argument contract plus paired exploit/control evidence rather than method-name widening.

**Final local acceptance.** The authoritative serial suite passes 1,172 tests and 8,875 assertions across 125 files with 19 intentional environment/platform skips and no failures. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, clean production build, and production advisory audit pass. The 251-entry npm archive passes strict path, duplicate, metadata, internal-reference, executable, and complete 79-file plugin-contract checks. Three isolated Windows installations and one Ubuntu installation under supported Node 22.23.1 validate public import and CLI behavior. The removed 1,554,638-byte archive had SHA-1 `f1744b80d2eed6782e9ac56fa14f889510c0535c` and SHA-256 `45588f94736c6d5343763726f7e78aefcabcf903814c65071beb5f1904fd1c80`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `258ce6d416fe6e7e9b4eb6e2168bb19a5c97372fd16d4b49c6dddcd626a31247`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores under a Linux-only path, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

## 2026-08-06 — Parse the complete Mongoose bulk-write grammar

**Decision.** Add `node-http-mongoose-bulk-write` as a separate exact host model rather than teaching the single-operation selector and update models to guess through an operation array. Reuse the official default, namespace, named-`model`, and CommonJS factory proof plus the unique non-reassigned Model identity. Parse argument zero to exact Model `bulkWrite()`, resolve bounded variable aliases, split literal arrays and objects structurally, and trace only documented nested data positions: `insertOne.document`; `updateOne.filter` and `updateOne.update`; `updateMany.filter` and `updateMany.update`; `deleteOne.filter`; `deleteMany.filter`; `replaceOne.filter`; and `replaceOne.replacement`. Treat a directly tainted whole array, dynamic array element, operation-object spread, or operation-specification spread as structural control because it can choose those positions.

**Why.** Mongoose documents `Model.bulkWrite()` as an operation array with distinct filter, update, replacement, insert, and option fields. It states that update entries contain update operators, that replacement entries carry a replacement document, and that validation defaults differ: `insertOne` and `replaceOne` are validated by default while updates are not equivalently protected ([Mongoose Model API](<https://mongoosejs.com/docs/api/model.html#Model.bulkWrite()>)). MongoDB likewise defines bulk `updateOne` and `updateMany` with separate filter and update documents, and permits update documents or pipelines ([MongoDB `Mongo.bulkWrite()`](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/v8.2/reference/method/mongo.bulkwrite/)). CodeQL's JavaScript modeling contract has a dedicated `nosql-injection` sink kind, supporting CWE-943 where input selects query grammar, while CWE-915 is more exact for unauthorized insert, replacement, or fixed-grammar field binding ([CodeQL custom JavaScript library models](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/customizing-library-models-for-javascript/)). A method-name or array-wide co-occurrence rule would conflate options, collation, array filters, and logging with executable selector or document grammar.

**Execution and classification.** `bulkWrite()` starts work when called and returns a Promise; it is not a lazy Mongoose Query. The model therefore requires the exact call but not `exec`, `await`, `then`, or `catch` to prove dispatch. It carries CWE-943 and CWE-915 as reviewer choices because one array can mix filters, update operators, inserts, and replacements. The quality gate requires CWE-943 only for proven filter or update query-language control and CWE-915 for unintended document fields or field-only updates, then requires proof of selected documents, one-versus-many behavior, authorization, protected fields, upserts, ordered or partial completion, validation and middleware, session and transaction state, write acknowledgement, database authority, and concrete impact.

**Counterevidence.** Apply controls to the exact nested position rather than the surrounding operation. An exact `$eq` boundary or exact official `mongoose.sanitizeFilter` can constrain the same complete filter. A fixed server-owned `$set` field can constrain a tainted update scalar. A literal insert or replacement object can constrain mass assignment only when each tainted occurrence is the value of an explicit fixed field; computed fields, spreads, and complete request objects receive no document-projection credit. Bulk options, `arrayFilters`, collation, timestamps, comments, schema casting, strict mode, validation, middleware, authentication, and denylists are not universal grammar or field-authorization controls.

**Red/green boundary.** Before implementation, the manifest-shape test passed and all four behavior and reviewer tests failed with zero `node-http-mongoose-bulk-write` rows and absent guidance. The first green boundary passes 16 adjacent Mongoose tests and 104 assertions. The four executable fixtures each retain nine ordered relative-import/call/parameter propagators to `src/storage.js:12`; only the two fixed controls carry their position-specific counterevidence. Nine adversarial direct cases cover every documented data position plus whole-array and spread control, while options-only, `arrayFilters`-only, fixed-only, lookalike, and reassigned cases emit no row. Six control cases remain reviewable, but only exact `$eq`, fixed `$set`, and fixed document projection receive candidate controls. TypeScript checking and all four live witnesses pass.

**Whole-repository prioritization boundary.** The first compiled self-review was byte-stable at 96 rows in 12,795 and 10,397 ms, but it was rejected: the unsafe replacement plus both controlled fixtures survived while the unsafe bulk update disappeared. Both dangerous paths had the same generic sink category, so category seeding selected only the lexically earlier replacement; the fixed controls still contributed unique control categories. The correction gives filter, update, insert, replacement, and unknown whole-array positions distinct sink kinds, narrows filter and document rows to CWE-943 or CWE-915 respectively, retains both IDs for update grammar and unknown arrays, and ranks only the two established bulk fixed boundaries behind unmitigated bulk rows. It does not reorder arbitrary candidate controls. A red ranking regression first observed the safe replacement ahead of both unsafe files; it now requires both dangerous operation kinds first, exact sink kinds, and exact CWE lists.

**Compiled self-review.** After the correction, two production-build passes take 12,959 and 12,624 ms, emit 96 byte-identical rows with SHA-256 `5e9b13c9d0debb73983a1ff7d80ae6e5aa6362797f575565feeab98c7311f9c8`, and retain all four fixtures. The unmitigated replacement is `mongoose-bulk-replacement-document` with CWE-915; the unmitigated update is `mongoose-bulk-update-document` with CWE-943 and CWE-915; their controlled twins retain the same position-specific identity plus only their exact fixed boundary. The adjacent bulk/update/inventory lane passes 77 tests and 1,128 assertions with one intentional symlink skip, and TypeScript checking passes.

**Final acceptance.** The authoritative serial run passes 1,166 tests and 8,813 assertions across 124 files with 19 intentional environment/platform skips and no failures. A first broad run was explicitly rejected even though 1,161 tests passed: the clean production build had been launched concurrently and removed `dist/benchmark-campaign.js` while four benchmark-runner recovery tests imported it. After build completion, the serial suite passed, and the final exact-code-head run repeated that result. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, clean build, and the production advisory audit pass. The 251-entry npm archive passes strict path, duplicate, metadata, internal-reference, executable, and complete 79-file plugin-contract checks. Two isolated Windows installs and one Ubuntu install under the existing supported Node 22.23.1 runtime validate import and CLI behavior. The first Ubuntu attempt used system Node 18.19.1 and was rejected at import because that unsupported runtime lacks `node:zlib` `crc32`; no engine floor was weakened. The removed 1,533,451-byte archive had SHA-1 `c692f5c6f58518c1c6648b66129f490ab0b14b44` and SHA-256 `a32f4d459fa41c163e44912500af45c4bd7cdc28d5d80c855f5ac597480c858c`.

**Desktop and hosted acceptance.** Windows builds with no warnings or errors, passes seven core and three shared tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `58528f73e9d24b5a9009813da107abe46a0a5a7fd5408821d097fd18f9ee31ef`. WSL Ubuntu uses a Linux-only runtime `PATH`, performs locked restores, builds with no warnings or errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes both non-graphical startup and the real Avalonia window under Xvfb. The 72,568-byte Linux executable retains SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`. Public exact-head runs `31116450445`, `31116450648`, `31116451062`, `31116454533`, `31116450772`, `31116450552`, and `31116451203` all pass at code checkpoint `6fd59b2f21c27ea7d37ec8a30d92798d67c9b5b3` for container, .NET, Go, Java, Linux GUI, Node, and Windows GUI respectively.

**Consequence.** Bulk selector injection, update-operator injection, replacement over-posting, and insertion over-posting now survive the same three-boundary graph without weakening the exact single-operation models. Deliberately unsupported surfaces remain dynamically selected method names, helpers that synthesize operation objects beyond the bounded alias resolver, MongoDB driver Collection identity, query-chain overloads, and flows beyond three relative-import boundaries. Those need separate exact contracts rather than relaxation of Model identity or nested-position proof.

## 2026-08-06 — Separate Mongoose update grammar from selectors

**Decision.** Add `node-http-mongoose-update` as a distinct host model instead of broadening `node-http-mongoose-nosql`. A candidate must resolve the same exact official Mongoose factory and unique non-reassigned Model identity, prove execution, and place request-derived data in the documented update argument of Model `updateOne`, `updateMany`, `findOneAndUpdate`, or `findByIdAndUpdate`. Operator or query-grammar control is classified as CWE-943; unintended attacker-selected fields without grammar control are classified as CWE-915. The deterministic row carries both IDs so the model reviewer must choose the concrete runtime effect.

**Why.** MongoDB accepts either an update-operator document or an aggregation pipeline in the update position, and operators including `$set`, `$unset`, `$rename`, and `$inc` change write semantics rather than acting as inert data ([MongoDB `updateOne`](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/reference/method/db.collection.updateone/), [MongoDB update operators](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/reference/mql/update/), [MongoDB `$unset`](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/manual/reference/operator/update/unset/)). Mongoose documents the Model methods as filter, update, and options positions; it also treats top-level non-operator update keys as `$set` while update pipelines are not cast ([Mongoose Model API](https://mongoosejs.com/docs/api/model.html)). Consequently, the selector model cannot safely infer this flow from argument zero, and a method-name-only matcher would confuse filters, options, replacement APIs, and query-chain overloads. CodeQL exposes `nosql-injection` as a dedicated JavaScript sink kind, supporting query-language classification when the attacker controls update grammar ([CodeQL custom JavaScript library models](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/customizing-library-models-for-javascript/)). CWE-915 remains the more exact classification when only unauthorized object attributes are writable.

**Exact position and consumption.** Reuse the official default, namespace, named `model`, and CommonJS binding proof from the selector model. The Model declaration must precede the sink, both factory and Model must remain unmodified, and a wrapper parameter cannot masquerade as the Model. Extract only argument one and require `await`, `exec`, `then`, `catch`, or return from an async exported wrapper. Deliberately reject replacement APIs and one-argument calls. A query-chain `Query#findOneAndUpdate(update)` has a different argument position and remains outside this exact Model model until its receiver identity and overload can be proved independently.

**Counterevidence.** Retain `fixed-update-field-value-boundary` only when every tainted wrapper parameter occurs as the scalar value of a fixed literal field beneath a server-owned `$set` object. A complete request object beneath `$set`, computed field, spread, tainted data outside that `$set`, denylist, or same-named helper does not qualify. `mongoose.sanitizeFilter` governs filters rather than updates. Mongoose update validators are disabled by default and, even when `runValidators` is enabled, apply only to selected operators and updated paths; `$inc` is among the documented exceptions ([Mongoose validation](https://mongoosejs.com/docs/validation.html#update-validators)). Casting, strict mode, middleware, authentication, and `requireFilter` therefore remain review leads rather than universal update grammar or field-authorization controls.

**Red/green boundary.** The strict red run passed only the manifest shape and failed the other four tests because no update row or reviewer guidance existed. After implementation and ranking hardening, 6 tests and 32 assertions pass. The four official binding/operation positives are the only rows in the position matrix; filter-only, options-only, replacement, one-argument, lookalike, and inert cases emit none. All five adversarial control cases remain reviewable, but only the fixed `$set` field receives counterevidence. The real positive preserves nine ordered relative-import/call/parameter propagators to `src/storage.js:12` with no candidate control; the matched control retains the same row and adds only the fixed-field boundary. Its witnesses prove that `{ $unset: { mfaSecret: 1 } }` removes the MFA secret in the vulnerable path and cannot cross the fixed-field path.

**Whole-repository prioritization boundary.** The first compiled self-review was deterministic and fast but retained only the safe update fixture at the global 96-row cap. Because that row sorted first and contributed the unique fixed-control category, category seeding selected it while the otherwise identical dangerous category was already represented. That acceptance result was rejected. Equal-priority records carrying `fixed-update-field-value-boundary` now sort behind a matching unmitigated record; category diversity still retains the controlled twin, so both review and counterevidence survive. A first general rule that ranked every record by candidate-control count was also rejected: the authoritative suite exposed three deterministic ordering changes in GORM expression/clause rows, pgconn query/COPY rows, and Java static `Path` factory specializations. Those candidate controls include binding and incomplete-risk evidence rather than established mitigation. The final comparator recognizes only the exact fixed update boundary, and a dedicated adversarial regression places a lexically earlier controlled file against a later dangerous file. The adjacent update, saturation, GORM, pgconn, and Java path matrix passes 135 tests and 1,438 assertions with one intentional symlink skip.

**Acceptance evidence.** The authoritative suite passes 1,160 tests and 8,769 assertions across 123 files with 19 intentional environment/platform skips and no failures. The clean production build emits 96 byte-identical self-inventory rows in 13,254 and 12,407 ms with SHA-256 `1b21c3c5a9e770db2b337bf520d53f9868324333a77ffa1f7c14aff23a66139e`; the unmitigated update row precedes the controlled twin, and no other update row appears.
