Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, and the production advisory audit pass with no known vulnerabilities. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries, and three isolated Windows/Linux installs validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The final local tarball is 1,544,434 bytes with SHA-1 `40971c8d3ee856702930ac3435ee4d20fbaa05f2` and SHA-256 `6a1015d85f026efca7044d49476d968a16ef5bff1faef5c15439bf46c829af27` before removal. The first local checker invocation deliberately failed only because release-only `gitHead` enforcement was mistakenly enabled against a local package that correctly omits release provenance; the ordinary local contract and all three installs passed. Windows builds with zero warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `921e75e15d360df15fe0d84d81cce14580d24523fd4eebf7cdc343558d3a7309`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores under a Linux-only `PATH`, builds with zero warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two headless Linux tests, and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Consequence.** Selector and update semantics can evolve independently without position confusion, and the reviewer receives explicit obligations for document selection, tenant/object authorization, sensitive fields, upserts, validators, middleware, immutable and discriminator keys, pipeline behavior, write outcome, and database authority. The bounded model intentionally misses query-chain overloads, bulk-write operation arrays, replacement-document over-posting, aggregation stages assembled through helpers, dynamically selected Model methods, and flows beyond three import boundaries. Those need their own exact receiver/position contracts rather than relaxation of this model.

## 2026-08-06 — Model exact Mongoose selector injection

**Decision.** Add a standalone `node-http-mongoose-nosql` host model for document-selector injection rather than relying on the residual prompt's generic NoSQL co-occurrence search. A candidate must resolve an official `mongoose` binding, prove that a unique local Model was constructed through the exact still-unmodified binding, place a request-derived value in the documented filter argument of a supported Model query, and prove that the query is consumed. Classify the broken data/query-grammar boundary as CWE-943.

**Why.** MongoDB filter objects are executable query structure, not merely scalar values: an object such as `{ $ne: null }` changes selection semantics. CodeQL's JavaScript query help explicitly treats object-valued NoSQL input as query-language injection and recommends either an exact primitive/literal boundary or `$eq` around untrusted values ([CodeQL JavaScript database-query injection](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-sql-injection/)). CodeQL's extensible JavaScript library models expose a dedicated `nosql-injection` sink kind and exact argument positions, and its standard library models cover MongoDB and Mongoose query objects ([custom JavaScript library models](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/customizing-library-models-for-javascript/), [CodeQL JavaScript library](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/codeql-library-for-javascript/)). Mongoose documents Model queries separately from their update, projection, and options arguments, while MongoDB's Node driver describes `findOne` as selecting the first document matching its query filter ([Mongoose Model API](https://mongoosejs.com/docs/api/model.html), [MongoDB `findOne`](https://www.mongodb.com/docs/drivers/node/v6.13/usage-examples/findOne/)). A method-name-only match would therefore overtaint update bodies and unrelated objects while missing the semantic operator boundary.

**Identity, position, and execution proof.** Accept default and namespace ESM receivers, whole-module CommonJS receivers, and named ESM/CommonJS `model` aliases. A receiver form must call its exact `.model(...)` member; a named function form must be invoked directly. The factory declaration must precede the model, cannot be reassigned before construction, and the resulting model name must be unique, cannot be a wrapper parameter, and cannot be reassigned before the sink. Only argument zero is extracted from `countDocuments`, `deleteMany`, `deleteOne`, `exists`, `find`, `findOne`, `findOneAndDelete`, `findOneAndReplace`, `findOneAndUpdate`, `replaceOne`, `updateMany`, and `updateOne`. The existing bounded Node summary graph then preserves that exact filter expression through as many as three relative-import call/parameter boundaries. A query is eligible only when the sink line awaits it, explicitly consumes it through `exec`, `then`, or `catch`, or returns the thenable from an async exported wrapper. A bare return from a synchronous wrapper is inert and rejected.

**Counterevidence.** An exact `$eq` boundary around the request-derived selector value is retained as `literal-query-value-equality`. An exact `mongoose.sanitizeFilter(...)` call through the proven official receiver is retained as `mongoose-filter-sanitization`; a same-named local helper is deliberately not trusted. Mongoose documents `sanitizeFilter` as wrapping nested properties whose names begin with `$` in `$eq` and exposes the option as opt-in rather than a universal default ([Mongoose API](<https://mongoosejs.com/docs/api/mongoose.html#Mongoose.prototype.sanitizeFilter()>)). The quality gate therefore requires the control to dominate the same complete filter and every effective query path. Casting, `strictQuery`, `runValidators`, `requireFilter`, authentication, projection, and result limiting do not independently establish a literal-value boundary.

**Adversarial and executable evidence.** The strict pair uses Express route → gateway → service → storage and requires all nine ordered import/call/parameter propagators. Its vulnerable storage query places the request value directly under `username`; the witness evaluates a representative `$ne` selector and selects the administrator record. The matched control changes only the selector boundary to `$eq`; its witness rejects the operator object and retains literal `guest` lookup. Synthetic cases accept default, namespace, named-alias, and CommonJS model construction while excluding data confined to update or options positions, local Model lookalikes, calling the Mongoose receiver without `.model`, reassigned factories, synchronous inert returned queries, and local sanitizer lookalikes. The first red run retained the strict manifest but emitted no exact framework rows and lacked the quality-gate instruction; the implemented model then passed all focused expectations without relaxing a gate.

**Performance correction.** The first compiled double self-review was deterministic but took 33,966 and 32,009 ms, outside the preceding scanner's 19–22 second envelope. A CPU profile attributed most samples to repeated JavaScript comment/string masking, structural-line construction, and exported-wrapper discovery. The graph was recalculating the same immutable source snapshot across same-file, terminal-wrapper, and relay stages, and exported JavaScript discovery separately remasked a thirteen-line slice for every source line. The corrected implementation materializes one structural array per snapshot and weakly caches JavaScript code lines, JavaScript/Python structural lines, and exported JavaScript/Python wrapper summaries. Weak keys bind every value to the exact immutable line-array identity and permit the complete source snapshot to be collected after a scan; no cache crosses changed source bytes. The final compiled pair takes 12,424 and 12,271 ms, about 2.7 times faster than the rejected build and materially faster than the previous envelope, while retaining 96 byte-identical rows and SHA-256 `73cdbd3b376a4866ff6c119f6370a64908def3adb8c7f7656675a0ed99ed4fee`.

**Acceptance evidence.** The strengthened focused lane passes 5 tests and 31 assertions, the residual-inventory lane passes 65 tests and 1,052 assertions with one intentional symlink skip, and both executable witnesses pass. The authoritative optimized suite passes 1,154 tests and 8,737 assertions across 122 files with 19 intentional environment/platform skips and no failures. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, the clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass with no known vulnerabilities. Repeated compiled self-review contains only the two expected Mongoose benchmark-fixture hypotheses. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; three isolated Windows/Linux installs validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,541,238-byte archive had SHA-1 `4b82e1f13fb7055e7038fe559d82e701c27b16a3` and SHA-256 `1de79f567bf8586c1e5cc67adf8777bff835775b13682c65e22c6e76ed94ba34`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `aa14cc0e68a317cdf4b6b8c7e0ce4244464f307a5fbf7ee0464a0b045c685372`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Failure boundary.** Arbitrary Mongoose re-exports, discriminators, dynamically selected or computed Model names, query-helper plugins, aggregate pipelines, `bulkWrite`, update-operator and field-name injection, compiler-resolved aliases beyond the accepted binding forms, callback-only execution, and runtime monkey patching remain outside this exact host proof. The broad residual search and mandatory model correction pass still receive those surfaces. Any future expansion must add an API-specific argument contract, an execution boundary, and paired exploit/control evidence rather than broadening the method regex alone.

## 2026-08-06 — Model exact Node and Python filesystem path positions

**Decision.** Add standalone `node-http-path` and `python-web-path` host models that resolve official filesystem bindings before tracing data and that attach taint only to each API's documented path-bearing arguments. Preserve the existing three-boundary relative-import graph, but do not let an operation-name regex establish ownership. For Node, accept named ESM and CommonJS aliases, namespace and default ESM receivers, CommonJS receivers/destructuring, promises variants, and direct official `require` receivers. For Python, accept an unshadowed builtin `open`, exact `builtins`, `os`, and `shutil` module aliases, and exact named aliases. Reject foreign modules, local lookalikes, parameter shadows, and binding reassignment.

**Why.** CodeQL's JavaScript and Python path-injection queries treat attacker-controlled filesystem paths as CWE-22-family risks spanning unauthorized reads, writes, and deletes ([JavaScript path injection](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-path-injection/), [Python path injection](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/python/py-path-injection/)). Its JavaScript library-model format also identifies the precise input position of a path-injection sink rather than assigning taint to an entire call ([JavaScript library models](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/customizing-library-models-for-javascript/)). Node documents the `path` or `filename` position of its filesystem operations and gives copy/rename/link-like calls distinct source and destination paths ([Node filesystem API](https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html)); Python similarly defines the first `open(file, ...)` argument as the path-like object and gives `shutil.copyfile(src, dst)` two path roles ([Python `open`](https://docs.python.org/3/library/functions.html#open), [Python `shutil`](https://docs.python.org/3/library/shutil.html)). Whole-call taint would therefore create an avoidable false positive whenever request data is only file contents, flags, mode, options, callback data, or encoding.

**Exact binding and position proof.** A candidate line is materialized only after the host resolves one supported operation through one official binding and extracts every documented path position available at that call. Node single-path operations use argument zero; copy, cp, link, rename, and symlink families use arguments zero and one. Python builtin `open` uses argument zero; supported `os` operations use their documented one- or two-path shape; `shutil` copy/move families use source and destination, while `rmtree` uses its first path. Dynamic alias calls are discovered from the resolved imports rather than from operation spelling alone. Binding declarations must precede the call, must not be replaced before it, and must not be shadowed by the containing exported wrapper parameter. The exact extracted expressions—not the complete call—drive same-file source matching and wrapper-parameter summaries. Bounded initializer recovery preserves a fixed-map selection such as `selected = DOCUMENTS[key]` or `DOCUMENTS.get(key)` through the terminal wrapper.

**Containment and counterevidence.** A server-owned key mapped to one fixed complete path is strong counterevidence and may be declared at module scope. Mere canonicalization or absolute-path rejection is not emitted as a standalone control lead unless component-aware containment is also present. Node documents lexical path operations separately from filesystem access ([Node path API](https://nodejs.org/api/path.html)); Python warns that `os.path.commonprefix` can return invalid paths and directs callers needing component-aware comparison to `commonpath` ([Python `os.path`](https://docs.python.org/3/library/os.path.html#os.path.commonprefix)). `path.resolve`, `os.path.join`, `normpath`, `abspath`, `realpath`, and `Path.resolve` remain construction or canonicalization evidence, not universal authorization: later absolute segments can reset an intended base, and lexical reasoning does not close symbolic-link, junction, mount, hard-link, writable-ancestor, or rename-race behavior. The quality gate must separately establish exact pre-sink dominance, decoding and separator behavior, volume syntax and case folding, tenant/object authorization, permissions, and the concrete read, disclosure, create, overwrite, delete, move, link, metadata, or served-content effect.

**Adversarial and executable evidence.** The strict lane contains two four-file positives and their topology-matched fixed-map controls. The Node positive reaches exact `node:fs/promises` `readFile`; the Python positive reaches the unshadowed builtin `open`; both carry the request through route, gateway, service, and storage and record three imports, calls, and parameters. Synthetic cases accept five Node binding shapes and five Python binding shapes, including taint in copy destination position, while rejecting request-controlled `writeFile` content, local Node lookalikes, a local Python `open`, and a foreign imported `open`. The regression also requires an official FastAPI import before `Path(...)` can be treated as a FastAPI-bound request source, preventing `pathlib.Path(...)` from creating a same-file duplicate. Real Node and WSL Ubuntu Python witnesses read their private parent fixture in each vulnerable case; both controls reject the traversal key and retain the public `welcome` read.

**Performance correction.** The red-to-green implementation initially invoked full binding and exported-wrapper discovery from every call-bearing line. That preserved correctness on fixtures but made a compiled inventory of the SDK take roughly 70–75 seconds per pass and caused the first double self-scan to exceed its three-minute command bound. Acceptance rejected that implementation. The final design first admits only lines containing a supported operation or an exact official named alias, then caches immutable Node binding/wrapper context in a `WeakMap` keyed by the source-line array. The cache neither crosses source snapshots nor retains them after the inventory graph is released. The same compiled self-scan now takes 22,257 ms and 19,425 ms for consecutive passes—about a threefold reduction—while producing the same 96 rows, zero Node/Python filesystem-path rows, byte equality, and SHA-256 `23822919ec67fc4302c030d55a29c3097227139b11fdc91fadbb84d73f26e249`.

**Acceptance evidence.** The focused lane passes 5 tests and 32 assertions. The authoritative post-cache suite passes 1,149 tests and 8,706 assertions across 121 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, and the clean production build pass. The pnpm production advisory audit reports no known vulnerabilities. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; three isolated Windows/Linux installs validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,518,640-byte archive had SHA-1 `f328aa0a1b2bf2ed5173c833e51dcfabed7bd2e4` and SHA-256 `3260a0e7faa4d616ec78982371af383cd106c1f1be9d1050efc6488861b84040`.

**Failure boundary.** Runtime monkey patching, computed property names, arbitrary re-exports, star or dot imports, Python keyword-only remapping, callbacks, custom filesystem wrappers, file-descriptor provenance, archive extraction, upload placement, and full filesystem race/link resolution remain outside this exact host proof. Unsupported or ambiguous binding syntax suppresses only the deterministic framework record; the model still receives the broader residual search and must validate any independently discovered path risk. Future expansion should add paired exploit/control evidence and an exact binding/argument contract rather than widening the operation-name regex.

## 2026-08-06 — Compose three exact Node and Python import boundaries

**Decision.** Extend the Node/TypeScript and Python framework summary graph from one relay layer to a hard maximum of two relay layers before the terminal sink wrapper. A request source may therefore preserve one value through as many as three exact call/parameter boundaries. Build the graph iteratively from terminal wrappers, retain both relay depths for callers, and emit every import, call argument, and wrapper parameter in source-to-sink order.

**Why.** Route/gateway/service/wrapper layouts are common and the existing host already had exact repository-relative resolution, exported-symbol discovery, positional parameter binding, reassignment rejection, and language-aware masking. The old one-relay ceiling therefore dropped an otherwise fully proved outer gateway. CodeQL distinguishes local from global interprocedural flow and uses summaries to synthesize flow through calls ([data-flow analysis](https://codeql.github.com/docs/writing-codeql-queries/about-data-flow-analysis/)). Node defines relative ESM specifiers as paths relative to the importing file and requires explicit file extensions ([Node ESM import specifiers](https://nodejs.org/api/esm.html#import-specifiers)); Python gives leading-dot imports package-relative meaning ([Python import system](https://docs.python.org/3/reference/import.html#package-relative-imports)). Those exact syntactic boundaries support one additional deterministic composition layer without accepting bare Node packages, ambiguous absolute Python imports, re-export inference, or runtime loader behavior.

**Bounded graph proof.** Start with existing sink wrappers, derive one relay layer from them, then derive one more layer only from the first. Each Node edge must resolve through the existing repository-relative ESM/CommonJS resolver to the exact imported export. Each Python edge must remain an explicit relative `from` import resolving to one public module-level function. At every layer, bind the exact positional argument to the exact exported parameter, require the call to remain inside that wrapper, and reject parameter reassignment before the call. Chain materialization rejects repeated source paths or malformed recursion and cannot exceed two relay nodes. Controls are collected from every relay and the terminal wrapper; the record key includes every relay file, call line, and parameter position so distinct exact paths do not collide.

**Adversarial and regression evidence.** The strict Node and Python command/SQL exploit-control fixtures now use route → gateway → service → sink-wrapper topologies. The red baseline lost exactly the two vulnerable command expectations and retained all existing safe behavior. After composition, each vulnerable command and SQL case emits one model with three imports, three calls, three parameters, and no duplicate path; both parameterized-SQL controls retain their exact binding lead, while both shell-free command controls remain outside the dangerous sink model. Synthetic controls reject reassignment in the newly added outer gateway, a deliberately over-depth route → gateway → facade → service → wrapper path, and a repeated-file service/runner cycle in both languages. Seven focused tests pass 59 assertions.

**Failure boundary.** A fourth import hop, Node package exports and loader hooks, dynamic imports, Python absolute imports, re-exports, star imports, keyword remapping, decorators, monkey patching, callbacks, transformed values, and runtime dispatch remain unsupported. Ambiguity, reassignment at any relay, repeated files, or an over-depth chain suppresses only that composed multi-hop record. Extending the cap again requires measured false-negative evidence plus paired cycle, ambiguity, reassignment, and performance controls.

**Acceptance evidence.** The widened Node/Python framework and residual-inventory matrix passes 122 tests and 1,409 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,144 tests and 8,674 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. Compiled self-review emits 96 byte-identical rows across repeated runs with SHA-256 `58e405b01f27bcad52e3155b87f76451d69d54536c08da064d5b8297de1a11bd`. Each vulnerable command and SQL fixture emits exactly one three-boundary model with nine ordered propagators; both shell-free command controls emit no dangerous model, and each parameterized SQL control retains one exact binding lead. Node syntax checks and its live SQLite grammar/binding witness pass. WSL Python syntax checks and the live SQLite exploit/control witness pass under an isolated bytecode cache.

Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, the clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass with no known vulnerabilities. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; three isolated Windows/Linux installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The Linux package probe initially used Ubuntu's unsupported `/usr/bin/node` 18.19.1 and failed on the Node 22 `node:zlib` `crc32` export; the declared and installed `/usr/local/bin/node` 22.23.1 passed without weakening the engine contract. The removed 1,522,403-byte archive had SHA-1 `444816fbfd4e8dbce2821e28b6125cfab0aedb4d` and SHA-256 `8cfc4b83f459af9b313950968a8aacc4374805177065581dd7af40ff88894960`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `40cd6f979a159568b44b875c7c59d1a3b867fe530b2a8350918288a2023dac52`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores under a Linux-only path, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Consequence.** Every existing Node/TypeScript and Python typed sink family gains the extra exact gateway layer without broadening source or sink recognition. All four language families now share a three-boundary host ceiling, while their language-specific identity proofs remain independent and auditable.

## 2026-08-06 — Compose three exact Java and C# service boundaries

**Decision.** Extend the existing typed Java and C# framework summary graph from one relay layer to a hard maximum of two relay layers before the terminal sink wrapper. A controller may therefore preserve one request value through as many as three uniquely typed service boundaries. Build the graph iteratively from terminal wrappers, retain both depths for callers, and emit every receiver binding, call argument, and wrapper parameter in source-to-sink order. Keep the Node/TypeScript and Python import graph at its existing two-hop boundary.

**Why.** Controller/facade/service/store layouts are ordinary in Spring and ASP.NET applications, but the previous controller/service/store ceiling dropped the exact outer facade flow even when every type, argument, and parameter was unambiguous. CodeQL distinguishes inexpensive local flow from more costly global interprocedural flow and describes summaries as the mechanism that synthesizes flow through calls ([data-flow analysis](https://codeql.github.com/docs/writing-codeql-queries/about-data-flow-analysis/), [Java/Kotlin library models](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/customizing-library-models-for-java-and-kotlin/)). This scanner does not have CodeQL's compiler database, so a small compositional graph with explicit identity checks is a defensible recall improvement; an unbounded name-based traversal is not. Java method-invocation identity and C# invocation binding are compile-time questions ([JLS 15.12](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/specs/jls/se21/html/jls-15.html#jls-15.12), [C# invocation expressions](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/language-specification/expressions#1264-invocation-expressions)), which is why unresolved, duplicated, or unsupported dispatch remains fail-closed.

**Bounded graph proof.** Start with existing typed sink wrappers, derive exactly one relay layer from them, then derive exactly one more layer only from the first. A candidate relay must still resolve its receiver owner to one source file, bind the exact positional argument to the exact downstream parameter under exact arity, preserve that parameter without reassignment before the call, and remain inside a parsed eligible method. Chain materialization rejects a repeated file path or malformed recursive summary and cannot exceed two relay nodes. Controls are collected from every relay plus the terminal wrapper; the terminal sink retains its original model, line, parameter position, and, for Spring mass assignment, domain-value type. The record key contains every relay file, call line, and parameter position so distinct exact paths neither collide nor duplicate.

**Adversarial and regression evidence.** The real Java and ASP.NET path exploit/control fixtures now use four files and the exact controller → `DocumentFacade` → `DocumentService` → `DocumentStore` topology. Both vulnerable fixtures retain the complete ordered source-to-sink propagator chain; the controls preserve their executable filesystem containment. The Java control additionally retains five exact host-authored control leads, while the transformed ASP.NET control remains outside the exact value-preserving path model. Synthetic controls reject reassignment at the newly added outer facade, duplicate receiver owners, fixed arguments, comments and strings, shadows, and deliberately over-depth Java and C# controller → gateway → facade → service → store chains. The red baseline missed only the two new three-boundary expectations; after graph composition, the paired focused suites pass 33 tests and 184 assertions.

**Failure boundary.** Interface and virtual dispatch, dependency-injection registrations, overload resolution, named arguments, Kotlin syntax, callbacks, reflection, transformed values, and a fourth service boundary remain unsupported. Ambiguity, reassignment at any recorded relay, repeated files, or an over-depth chain suppresses only that composed multi-hop record. Extending the cap again requires measured false-negative evidence and paired ambiguity, cycle, reassignment, and performance controls rather than a larger constant alone.

**Acceptance evidence.** All thirteen Java/.NET framework and inventory suites pass 160 tests and 1,538 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,142 tests and 8,662 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. Deterministic compiled inventory emits one exact three-boundary model for each vulnerable fixture, preserves all nine ordered propagators, attaches five containment leads only to the Java control, and leaves the production repository at 96 rows with neither path model. Both ASP.NET fixtures build with zero warnings/errors; both Spring fixtures compile under WSL Java 17; and all four witnesses prove the expected exploit/control outcomes. WSL's distribution Maven selected compiler-plugin 3.1, which ignores `maven.compiler.release`; the first plain local build therefore defaulted to Java 5, while the explicit source/target 17 retry compiled all four source files. Hosted modern-Maven CI remains the independent plain-command gate.

Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, the clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass with no known vulnerabilities. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; three isolated Windows/Linux installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,521,835-byte archive had SHA-1 `28e7701e8aae1e058e219657fd0355cd7bf65983` and SHA-256 `45c7b17d1308ec4fb95db28e7ed03d635e6353e822f5562ca5e3f5af3ab4f312`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `9bdc6f0469793dccd1c6b76ff7da235d592c0ef656d57bd42b1c74c594ac2d96`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`. The first Windows publish and package-install probes were rejected only because NuGet advisory metadata and the npm cache were unavailable inside the restricted filesystem/network sandbox; the approved retries used the ordinary host resources and passed.

**Consequence.** Every existing typed Java and C# sink family benefits from the additional exact facade layer through the shared summary graph, without broadening language-source syntax or sink recognition. The graph remains bounded, deterministic, cycle-free, and evidence-rich, while applications whose identity requires compiler or runtime dispatch continue to be handed to deeper review instead of receiving speculative host-authored flow.

## 2026-08-06 — Resolve exact direct Maven reactor dependencies

**Decision.** Extend only the Java basename-helper visibility graph with direct conventional Maven reactor dependencies. A caller can read a sibling only when its own top-level `dependencies` block contains literal `groupId`, `artifactId`, and `version` coordinates that identify exactly one module in exactly one retained reactor; the scope is absent, `compile`, or `provided`; the type is absent or `jar`; no classifier is present; and caller plus helper both remain under conventional `src/main/java`. Preserve direction and do not make the graph transitive.

**Why.** The Maven reactor [sorts a project dependency before its consumer](https://maven.apache.org/guides/mini/guide-multiple-modules.html), but `dependencyManagement` alone does not affect that order. Maven's [dependency mechanism](https://maven.apache.org/guides/introduction/introduction-to-dependency-mechanism.html) places `compile` dependencies on the production compile classpath and `provided` dependencies on the compile and test classpaths, while `runtime` and `test` do not establish production compilation. The previous nearest-POM partition correctly rejected undeclared siblings but also lost ordinary direct helper-library calls that the compiler can resolve. Recovering only the exact declared reactor edge improves recall without returning to repository-wide name matching.

**Bounded graph proof.** Parse only a deliberately small, non-executing XML surface: one `project` with exact model version 4.0.0, its direct coordinates and optional packaging, one exact local parent, literal `modules`, and direct top-level dependencies. Reject DTDs, CDATA, entity references, prefixed or otherwise unsupported XML syntax, misplaced or duplicate XML declarations, excessive nesting, duplicate structural fields, property expressions, and unsafe repository-relative paths. Resolve default or literal local parent POMs, require their declared coordinates to equal the parent's recursively effective coordinates, and inherit only group/version under that proof, following Maven's [POM inheritance rules](https://maven.apache.org/guides/introduction/introduction-to-the-pom.html). Form reactor closures only from literal module paths under `pom` aggregators; reject cycles, missing modules, duplicate membership, excessive depth, and any reactor overlapping another retained top-level reactor. Match a dependency against one exact effective GAV and ordinary JAR target, then union that direct edge with the existing Gradle graph. Malformed dependency entries are ignored individually, so one dynamic declaration cannot erase unrelated exact module membership or edges.

**Failure boundary.** Dependency management, profiles, plugin dependencies, BOM imports, properties, version ranges, repositories, classifiers, non-JAR types, system paths, test/runtime/import scopes, optional reactor substitution, transitive reachability, custom source roots, external-parent inheritance, model interpolation, Maven extensions, and executable build behavior remain unsupported. An unsupported declaration removes only its own edge. The broad Spring path hypothesis remains present, and helper/type ambiguity across the caller plus every proven direct dependency continues to reject specialization.

**Regression evidence.** A nested application with literal `../../pom.xml` inheritance consumes a `Path.getFileName()` helper under default scope; a second caller proves `provided`; and a third proves explicit `compile` with a `File.getName()` helper. Identical callers cover undeclared and reverse direction, `test`, `runtime`, `system`, and `import` scopes, version mismatch, property coordinates, dependency-management-only declaration, non-JAR type, classifier, transitive-only reachability, a helper outside `src/main/java`, and two visible modules exporting the same owner. All seventeen broad rows remain, while only the three exact direct production compile relationships specialize. Separate malformed-POM controls omit `modelVersion` or place an XML declaration inside `project`; both retain their broad path row and reject specialization. Reviewer guidance and user, benchmark, landscape, and bundled-plugin documentation describe the accepted proof and exclusions.

**Acceptance evidence.** The focused Java path lane passes 26 tests and 154 assertions. A widened six-suite Java/framework/inventory slice passes 103 tests and 1,273 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,142 tests and 8,662 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. Compiled self-review remains 96 deterministic rows with neither basename specialization. All four Maven fixtures compile; all four JDK witnesses preserve exact exploit/control behavior; and compiled deterministic review preserves one exact specialization per fixture, helper reduction at `DocumentNames.java:9`, and caller-side parent rejection only in the two controls. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, clean production build, and the production audit pass with no known vulnerabilities. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; three isolated installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,520,449-byte archive had SHA-1 `898e7e2ba729ea1d51bb4a08483f7968f64dfa81` and SHA-256 `6e582e67a5eb717aa33e62c5b292da73d341278d39de5f4eee521224ec275b1d`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `5c8b9fb1f05d24fd385a058b3ccaf197783b5fde362623273b4516285ee6e7ac`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores under a Linux-only path, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes both non-graphical and X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Consequence.** Conventional Maven multi-module applications recover exact direct basename-helper flows without invoking Maven or trusting its network, plugins, repositories, profiles, or arbitrary model-building behavior. Future support for properties, external parents, profiles, source-set plugins, or transitive classpaths needs a separately auditable effective-model proof and must preserve ambiguity rejection.

## 2026-08-06 — Follow literal Gradle compile project dependencies

**Decision.** Extend only the Java basename-helper project identity with a direct Gradle compile dependency graph. A caller can read its own module plus a target module named by an exact literal `api`, `implementation`, `compileOnly`, or `compileOnlyApi` `project(":path")` declaration in the caller's own top-level `dependencies` block. Resolve that path only through one retained `settings.gradle` or `settings.gradle.kts`, conventional colon-to-directory mapping, and exactly one standard build file at each endpoint. Do not make the graph transitive.

**Why.** The preceding exact module partition removed an undeclared-sibling false positive but necessarily introduced a false negative for the ordinary multi-project case where an application explicitly compiles against a shared helper project. Gradle's [multi-project build documentation](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/multi_project_builds.html) shows both Kotlin `implementation(project(":shared"))` and Groovy `implementation project(':shared')` project dependencies and states that project dependencies affect the consuming classpath and build order. Recovering that exact declared edge is materially stronger than either repository-wide name matching or permanent sibling isolation.

**Bounded graph proof.** Pair original Gradle lines with the existing comment/string-masked structural surface. Accept single-line literal `include(...)`/`include '...'` declarations and only direct statements in a top-level `dependencies` block. Map `:services:shared` to `services/shared`; require the mapped directory and caller directory to expose exactly one `build.gradle` or `build.gradle.kts`; require each physical root to belong to only one retained settings build; and preserve edge direction. Extend top-level-owner uniqueness and same-package shadow lookup across the caller root plus its direct target roots so dependency recovery cannot silently choose between duplicate visible classes.

**Failure boundary.** Test and runtime configurations do not establish the production compile identity used by this model. Variables, interpolation, named/dynamic `project` expressions, nested configuration blocks, transitive dependencies, source-set relocation, custom `projectDir` or `buildFileName`, composite `includeBuild`, multiple settings/build files, Gradle triple/dollar-slashy strings, and missing inventory files remain unsupported. Such constructs remove only the unproved edge; they do not suppress the broad Spring path hypothesis. A settings file may include an empty or unretained project without disabling exact edges between other fully retained conventional modules.

**Regression evidence.** Four Kotlin/Groovy applications reach a public `Path.getFileName()` helper through literal `implementation`, `api`, `compileOnly`, and `compileOnlyApi` project edges. A fifth Groovy application reaches a public `File.getName()` helper through literal `implementation project(':libraries:filelib')`, proving nested project-path mapping. Identical callers with no edge, the edge in the opposite direction, `testImplementation`, `runtimeOnly`, and a variable project name remain broad-only. A caller with two dependencies that expose the same package/type remains ambiguous and broad-only. A declared target whose helper is parked outside conventional `src/main/java` also remains broad-only. A nested settings build with custom `projectDir` remains broad-only, while an included module absent from the source inventory does not erase the independent proven edges. Reviewer guidance names the accepted compile configurations and every deliberate exclusion.

**Acceptance evidence.** The focused Java path lane passes 24 tests and 144 assertions. The widened six-file Java/framework/inventory slice passes 113 tests and 1,310 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,140 tests and 8,652 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. Compiled self-review remains 96 deterministic rows with neither basename specialization. All four Maven fixtures compile; all four JDK witnesses preserve exact exploit/control behavior; and compiled deterministic review preserves one exact specialization per fixture, helper reduction at `DocumentNames.java:9`, and caller-side parent rejection only in the two controls. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, clean production build, and the production audit pass with no known vulnerabilities. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; three isolated installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,494,486-byte archive had SHA-1 `1c05d226554d797d048a0f2170d8b029db536eb8` and SHA-256 `523360b2bb0c10c16fb9e9d92ea8a50acfff379fbbab983cd2e5945fe7166e40`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `93e4063dd4dabace479c1ac2d668c68043758f378660b0fb26ccbbeb619c14c6`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores under a Linux-only path, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes both non-graphical and X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Consequence.** The scanner recovers direct shared-module basename flows without executing Gradle or reopening arbitrary cross-module matching. Future expansion to aliases, version catalogs, custom layouts, source sets, build logic, or transitive classpaths needs an equally auditable identity and ambiguity proof; merely recognizing a project name is insufficient.

## 2026-08-06 — Partition Java basename identity by exact Gradle boundaries

**Decision.** Extend the project-local identity used by `File.getName()` and `Path.getFileName()` helper summaries and same-package shadow resolution from Maven-only `pom.xml` ancestry to the deepest exact `pom.xml`, `build.gradle`, `build.gradle.kts`, `settings.gradle`, or `settings.gradle.kts` ancestor. Add `.gradle` to the bounded source inventory; `.kts` is already source. Keep this Gradle-aware scope local to the basename specializations so unrelated Java models do not silently change their existing Maven dependency assumptions.

**Why.** A paired regression proved both error directions in a conventional Gradle monorepo. Flattening every non-Maven source under `.` caused a valid app-local `PathNames` helper to become ambiguous merely because an unrelated sibling declared the same package and type. The same flattening credited a lone `FileNames` helper from another sibling even though the caller declared no dependency and could not compile against it. Gradle's [multi-project build documentation](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/multi_project_builds.html) describes root projects and logically isolated subprojects, ordinarily with per-project `build.gradle(.kts)` files, while its [project-organization guidance](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/organizing_gradle_projects.html) identifies `settings.gradle(.kts)` as the build entry point and subproject structure definition. Treating those exact conventional files as lexical boundaries is stronger than repository-wide name matching without pretending to execute Gradle.

**Boundary proof.** Retain Gradle Groovy and Kotlin scripts in the same bounded, non-symlink source snapshot used by deterministic analysis. For each Java caller or helper, select the longest ancestor directory containing one of the five exact boundary filenames. Scope helper ownership, duplicate-type rejection, and wildcard-import shadow checks to that root. A subproject build file therefore isolates ordinary sibling modules, and a nested settings file isolates a composite/included build. If no marker is retained, preserve the existing repository-root fallback. Do not parse arbitrary settings code, `projectDir`, `buildFileName`, source-set relocation, included-build substitution, or `project(...)` dependency edges; a helper outside the selected root fails closed even if a real compiler could resolve it through a declared dependency.

**Regression evidence.** The deterministic fixture uses both `build.gradle` and `build.gradle.kts`, a Kotlin root settings file, and a nested Groovy settings file. One app-local helper remains eligible despite a same-package/type peer duplicate. One nested-build helper remains eligible despite the same package/type at the outer build. A controller that names a helper found only in an undeclared sibling retains its broad Spring path row but emits no basename specialization. Exact helper-file reduction evidence remains unchanged.

The six-file Java/framework/inventory slice passes 100 tests and 1,257 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,139 tests and 8,646 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. The compiled SDK self-inventory remains 96 deterministic rows with neither Java basename specialization. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; three isolated installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,506,189-byte archive had SHA-1 `edb525a102adb5fcf2c6260252472e6527ebc7af` and SHA-256 `8a19cf597e3137a09986ef5d3c5546554d8c8e9dd33996adbb9a5e4a6c899062`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Consequence.** The scanner removes one Gradle-specific false negative and one cross-module false positive while preserving the compiler-free, fail-closed identity contract. Future work may parse literal `include`, `projectDir`, `buildFileName`, and `project(":module")` dependency declarations to recover exact cross-module flows, but must reject dynamic settings/build logic, custom plugins, ambiguous composite substitution, and dependency configurations whose compile classpath role is not proven.

## 2026-08-05 — Resolve exact project-local Java basename helpers

**Decision.** Extend the bounded `File.getName()` and `Path.getFileName()` helper summaries across one Java compilation-unit boundary. Admit a call only when caller and helper share the nearest Maven project, exactly one non-test top-level owner exists in the helper package, the owner resolves through the caller's same package, one exact single-type import, or a fully qualified name, and the target method is static and accessible. A different package requires both a public top-level class or record and a public method. Retain the same exact official input/return types, unoverloaded symbol, straight-line return, fixed arity, argument position, and identifier identity required by the same-file proof. Record the reduction at the helper source while beginning dominance analysis at the caller assignment.

**Why.** A reproduced controller-to-filesystem flow retained its base `spring-http-path` record but lost the specialized basename warning solely because a small naming helper moved into a peer source file. That is a concrete false negative in an ordinary Java organization pattern. CodeQL's [Java/Kotlin library-model documentation](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/customizing-library-models-for-java-and-kotlin/) treats callable input-to-return summaries as explicit flow steps, while [JLS 15.12](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/specs/jls/se21/html/jls-15.html#jls-15.12) requires method-owner, accessibility, applicability, arity, and overload resolution. Without a compiler database, the scanner uses a smaller auditable identity proof rather than approximating arbitrary Java dispatch.

**Resolution and failure boundary.** Build summaries once from production Java files and bind each to its package, nearest Maven project, top-level owner, access, static modifier, exact parameter slot, and helper evidence path. Same-package package, protected, or public static methods are eligible. Cross-package calls require an exact import or fully qualified owner plus public owner and method. Wildcard custom imports never establish ownership. Private cross-file methods, instance receivers, duplicate top-level owners, overloads, nested-project helpers, unofficial or shadowed JDK types, transformed arguments, branches, helper chains, and ambiguous matches fail closed. Same-file private and instance-helper behavior remains separately bounded by the existing exact receiver rules.

**Evidence and regression boundary.** The specialized candidate control now points to the exact `DocumentNames.java` reduction line. An exact parent check is still searched only between the caller's helper-result assignment and sink, so helper-file line numbers cannot create false dominance. The focused matrix covers same-package package access, exact cross-package import, fully qualified cross-package ownership, both basename APIs, a caller-side guard, duplicate owners, overloads, private access, a non-public cross-package owner, transformed arguments, instance receivers, wildcard-import shadowing, and a nested Maven project. The four Spring basename fixtures now execute through a separate package-local helper compilation unit while their dependency-free JDK witnesses retain the same runtime oracle.
