WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux tests, and passes both non-graphical and X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Consequence.** The scanner recovers a common cross-file false-negative class without opening arbitrary call-graph propagation. Inheritance, virtual dispatch, interfaces, static imports of custom helpers, wildcard custom imports, multi-hop helper chains, varargs, generics, compiler-resolvable overloads, non-Maven project boundaries, and conditional but value-preserving implementations remain intentionally unsupported and must fail closed until an equally exact identity proof exists.

## 2026-08-05 — Summarize exact same-file Java basename helpers

**Decision.** Carry an already proven `spring-http-path` value through a same-file helper return before deriving `java-file-getname-path-boundary` or `java-path-getfilename-path-boundary`. Admit only one method declaration with that symbol, exact official `String`/`File` or `String`/`Path` input and return types, one straight-line return, an exact unqualified, `this`-qualified, or owner-type-qualified call, matching fixed arity, and the proven value in the summarized parameter position. Preserve the reduction's helper-body source line as evidence and allow the existing caller-side exact parent rejection to control the returned lineage.

**Why.** Real Java repositories routinely hide small normalization and naming operations behind local helpers. The base Spring graph already preserved the request through the helper call because the value still reached the typed filesystem sink, but both basename specializations reopened only the exported sink method and therefore missed the actual `getName` or `getFileName` expression in a private helper. That was a demonstrated false negative, not a hypothetical feature request. CodeQL likewise treats callable summaries as explicit input-to-return flow models in its [Java/Kotlin library-model documentation](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/customizing-library-models-for-java-and-kotlin/). Java method calls require owner, applicability, arity, and overload resolution under [JLS 15.12](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/specs/jls/se21/html/jls-15.html#jls-15.12); because this host has no compiler database, it accepts a deliberately smaller set that it can identify exactly and rejects every overloaded symbol rather than guessing the most-specific declaration.

**Bounded summary proof.** Parse outer-class member methods at lexical depth one, including non-exported helpers and exact parameter/return declarations. Structurally mask comments and literals, require exactly one terminal `return`, and reject nested blocks, lambdas, branches, loops, synchronization, abrupt statements, and trailing code. Within that straight-line prefix, propagate only exact identifier aliases, exact official `new File`, `Path.of`, or `Paths.get` constructions, exact basename reductions, and their result aliases; clear every lineage on reassignment or transformation. At the call site, require exact arity and an exact identifier argument from the already proven String/File/Path lineage. Only bare, `this.`, and exact owner-type receivers are eligible. Helper-to-helper calls, arbitrary object receivers, expressions in the summarized argument, overloads, unofficial types, tests/examples, and cross-file helpers fail closed.

**Declaration identity.** The executable fixture conversion exposed that a bounded multiline declaration window could rediscover the same method from a leading blank or annotation line. Collapse only discoveries with the same symbol, normalized return type, exact parameter declarations, and structural end line, retaining the latest declaration start so reduction line accounting remains exact. Different parameter declarations remain distinct; a true overload still makes every same-name summary ineligible.

**Executable and regression evidence.** Route the existing vulnerable and safe `File.getName()` and `Path.getFileName()` Spring repositories and all four pure-JDK witnesses through private same-file helpers without changing their payloads, sinks, or expected security behavior. The focused lane passes 20 tests and 122 assertions. Positive cases cover String, File, and Path inputs; direct construction/receiver returns; local aliases; multiple parameters; fully qualified official types despite a same-package `String`; caller-side exact parent rejection; and bare, `this`, and owner-qualified calls. Negative cases preserve the base `spring-http-path` proof while rejecting overloaded, branchy, transformed, reassigned, nested, foreign-receiver, and wildcard-shadowed helpers.

**Acceptance evidence.** The six-file Java/framework/inventory slice passes 109 tests and 1,288 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,136 tests and 8,630 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. All four Spring fixtures compile under Maven in WSL Ubuntu; the two vulnerable JDK witnesses escape with exact `..`, and both safe witnesses reject the same payload while preserving an ordinary name. Compiled production output emits the File positive at line 23 with only `incomplete-java-io-file-getname-reduction`, the File control at line 26 with exact parent rejection, the Path positive at line 28 with only `incomplete-java-nio-path-getfilename-reduction`, and the Path control at line 25 with exact parent rejection. The production SDK remains 96 deterministic rows and emits neither specialization. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, the clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; three isolated installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled plugin files. The removed 1,497,109-byte archive had SHA-1 `f23a96d35e82e16ef46be1608d39cd736a2c5f4a` and SHA-256 `43349afbde1b50ad723c5607dc5130d85ac809da1c7fefa65863affd936b7edf`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core plus three shared desktop tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `e1f663725ca8cde64c5b702ff70fc49ae0ed29adab78176b80a65e4b44ceb391`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`. The first Windows publication and npm audit/package-smoke attempts were rejected only because their respective NuGet/npm metadata or cache paths were unavailable inside the network/filesystem sandbox; the explicitly approved retries refreshed advisory data, used the ordinary user cache, and passed before the archive was removed.

**Consequence.** The scanner closes a concrete false-negative class without converting arbitrary local method calls into taint propagation. This intentionally misses cross-file helpers, recursion, helper chains, inherited or overridden dispatch, generics, varargs, conditional but value-preserving bodies, transformed arguments, and compiler-resolvable overloads. Those cases require a repository-wide symbol table or compiler-backed call graph and must not be enabled by relaxing exact owner, type, arity, argument, or straight-line identity.

## 2026-08-05 — Require branch-local abrupt completion for Java basename rejection

**Decision.** Attach `parent-path-component-rejection` to exact `java.io.File.getName()` and `java.nio.file.Path.getFileName()` lineages only when the exact parent equality is the complete positive condition, the matching branch itself unconditionally completes through `return` or `throw`, and that guard begins in the same lexical block path as the later filesystem sink. Apply the same rule to both basename specializations and to both direct and reverse equality forms. Preserve the specialized risk row when this proof is absent.

**Why.** The prior bounded matcher found an exact equality and then searched the next four source lines for any `return` or `throw`. That could credit a logging-only parent check merely because a later, unrelated state check threw. It could also misclassify a negated equality, a condition conjoined with an optional flag, a parent check nested under an optional outer branch, or a throw caught before the sink. Java executes an `if` branch only for the corresponding Boolean result, and `return`/`throw` prevent normal completion only along the statement that actually executes; [JLS Chapter 14](https://docs.oracle.com/javase/specs/jls/se21/html/jls-14.html) defines those normal and abrupt completion rules. CodeQL exposes the stronger formulation through Java [guard-control](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-standard-libraries/java/semmle/code/java/controlflow/Guards.qll/module.Guards.html) and [dominance](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-standard-libraries/java/semmle/code/java/controlflow/Dominance.qll/module.Dominance.html) predicates. This scanner has no compiler CFG, so adjacency was too weak and a conservative lexical proof is preferable to false counterevidence.

**Bounded control proof.** Mask comments and literals structurally while retaining the original condition for exact `".."`, `Path.of("..")`, and `Paths.get("..")` identity. Assign a stable nested block path and switch-ancestor flag from structural braces. Require the candidate `if` and sink to start in the same block path outside a switch block, which rejects a guard hidden in an optional outer block, a caught throw whose sink occurs after the enclosing `try`/`catch`, and a guard in a different `case` entry from the sink. Strip only balanced outer condition parentheses and anchor the whole condition to direct or reverse equality; negation, `&&`, `||`, and unrelated predicates therefore fail closed. For an unbraced branch, require its statement to begin with `return` or `throw`. For a braced branch, allow straight-line statements ending in `return` or `throw`, but reject nested braces, branches, loops, `try`/`catch`/`finally`, `switch`, synchronization, and lambdas. Do not treat `break` or `continue` as method-level rejection. Full Java CFG, exception-type resolution, Boolean implication, finally semantics, and helper summaries remain outside this host proof.

**Adversarial and executable evidence.** The positive `Path.getFileName()` Spring fixture now compares the exact reduced value with `Path.of("..")`, logs the match, and then performs an unrelated null-state throw check before `Files.readString`. The old four-line heuristic would have attached rejection; the new proof does not. Its pure-JDK witness logs the parent request and still reads the parent `content.txt`. The unchanged matched control terminates the exact parent branch and rejects both exact and nested parent names. Both Maven fixtures compile with Java 17 release semantics on WSL Java 21 and both witnesses execute with their expected exploit/control outcomes.

**Regression evidence.** Focused tests cover direct and reverse equality, static factories, a straight-line audit followed by abrupt completion, negated equality, conditional conjunction, optional nesting, caught throws, distinct switch cases, logging-only branches, unrelated nearby throws, another reduction, post-sink checks, and both `File` and `Path` lineages; 16 tests pass 106 assertions. The widened Java cross-file, path, framework, multi-hop, and residual-risk slice passes 105 tests and 1,272 assertions with one intentional platform skip. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, and the clean production build pass. Compiled inventory emits the positive specialization at `DocumentStore.java:24` with only `incomplete-java-nio-path-getfilename-reduction`, emits the matched control at line 21 with the additional `parent-path-component-rejection`, and emits no Java basename specialization from the production SDK's 96 deterministic records.

**Acceptance evidence.** The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,132 tests and 8,614 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. The production advisory audit reports no known vulnerabilities. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; two isolated installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled-plugin files. The removed 1,491,884-byte archive had SHA-256 `631674a674521d3e232baa67ed108e5b587612d485fb416e8bb3f766902834cf`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared tests, and publishes a fresh 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `2c0ffc204424ee15b9a226790796edf7ad9a458bde5edb4e2725fe765131660b`. WSL Ubuntu performs locked restores after the Windows publication, builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core, three shared, and two Linux interface tests, and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`. The first Windows publication probe could not reach NuGet advisory metadata inside the network sandbox and was not accepted; the explicit network-enabled retry refreshed and hashed the artifact.

**Consequence.** A syntactically impressive but noncontrolling check can no longer bias the model toward a false negative merely because abrupt syntax is nearby. This improves precision of control evidence while intentionally sacrificing recognition of complex but safe Boolean equivalences, helper predicates, `else`-branch rejection, and compiler-proven control flow. Future work may replace the bounded lexical proof with a compiler-backed CFG and exception graph, but must preserve exact reduced-value identity, branch polarity, sink dominance, and uncaught abrupt completion.

## 2026-08-05 — Resolve Java path factories by compilation-unit identity

**Decision.** Resolve simple `Path`, `Paths`, `of`, and `get` factory names under the Java compilation unit's import and package context before deriving `java-path-getfilename-path-boundary`. Preserve exact fully qualified factories. Treat a single-type `java.nio.file.Path` or `Paths` import as authoritative over a top-level same-package declaration, but allow an on-demand `java.nio.file.*` import only when the nearest Maven project has no top-level type with the same name in that package. Accept exact and on-demand static imports of `Path.of` and `Paths.get` only when no local owner method, qualified lookalike invocation, or competing same-name static import can own the unqualified call.

**Why.** The initial exact reduction deliberately rejected local `Path`/`Paths` declarations but did not inspect another compilation unit in the same package and did not recognize static factories. The Java 21 language rules distinguish these cases: a single-type import shadows a top-level same-package type declared in another compilation unit, while an on-demand import does not shadow it. CodeQL's Java library exposes resolved reference types, declaring methods, packages, and compilation units rather than inferring ownership from method spelling. This host has no compiler database, so it now implements the relevant identity slice explicitly and fails closed where static overload ownership would require full method resolution.

**Package and type boundary.** Parse the declared package from masked structural source. Build a brace-depth-zero surface to collect only top-level class, interface, enum, record, and annotation-interface names; a nested `Path` inside another class is not a package member and does not suppress a wildcard import in a sibling compilation unit. Restrict peers to the sink file's nearest `pom.xml` project, require the same declared package, and exclude test/example paths so test-only helpers cannot hide production identities. An exact single-type import stays eligible despite a peer name. A wildcard import loses to an exact same-project, same-package peer. A different package, nested declaration, nested Maven module, or fully qualified factory does not alter the binding.

**Static method boundary.** Normalize whitespace around selectors before matching. Support `import static java.nio.file.Path.of`, `Path.*`, `Paths.get`, and `Paths.*`, including exact parent equality through the same imported factory. Extract only unqualified calls; `Evil.of(path)` cannot fall through to the simple `of` pattern. Scan the outer type's brace-depth-one member surface for a local method declaration and reject the imported name when present. Reject another explicit same-name static import or another static wildcard because signature and overload resolution are unavailable without a compiler database. Preserve request argument, receiver/result alias, reassignment, reduction-origin, sink, and parent-rejection requirements unchanged.

**Regression evidence.** Fourteen focused tests pass 90 assertions. Positive cases cover exact `Path` and `Paths`, fully qualified factories, typed parameters, same-package single-type precedence, nested and different-package lookalikes, and all four exact/on-demand static `of`/`get` import forms. Negative cases cover same-package wildcard `Path` and `Paths` shadows, same-file shadows, qualified lookalike calls, local owner methods, competing static wildcards, unrelated reductions, fixed values, reassignment, tests, and cross-file parameter mismatch. The exact statically imported `of("..")` parent guard remains attached to the same reaching reduction, and prompt regressions preserve the import-precedence and static-owner boundary for the model reviewer.

**Acceptance evidence.** The widened Java, Spring, framework, and residual-risk slice passes 124 tests and 1,344 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative suite passes 1,130 tests and 8,598 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. Compiled review preserves the exact positive specialization with only the incomplete-reduction lead, the exact control specialization with parent rejection, and zero specializations from the production SDK's 96 deterministic rows. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, the clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; two isolated installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled-plugin files. The removed 1,488,545-byte archive had SHA-256 `685125d6820580614843fc2085ae86626eede0e0642a7d2bebf24b6e9d4e8ca3`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared tests, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `31a2439db5e1994f5c1b006c6accc7048aeef3d3a06507cf5786683de282a296`. WSL Ubuntu builds without warnings/errors; passes seven core, three shared, and two headless tests; and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb startup. Its 72,568-byte executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`. A first Linux no-restore probe correctly failed after the Windows publish rewrote shared `obj` assets to Windows NuGet cache paths; repeating the locked Linux restores regenerated the platform-specific graph and the unchanged source then passed every Linux gate. Hosted jobs use isolated checkouts and do not share this local cross-OS artifact boundary.

**Consequence.** The scanner gains static-import recall and removes a repository-wide wildcard-import false-positive class without treating every matching filename as a shadow. Full overload resolution, module-path dependencies, annotation processors, generated sources, non-Maven source sets, static-import signature compatibility, and compiler-error recovery remain outside this bounded host model and must continue to fail closed rather than be guessed.

## 2026-08-05 — Keep exact `Path.getFileName()` parent names tainted

**Decision.** Derive `java-path-getfilename-path-boundary` only from an existing typed `spring-http-path` record. Reopen the exact Java sink method, require official imported or fully qualified `java.nio.file.Path` or `Paths` identity, and trace only the already proven wrapper parameter or exact same-file source through `Path.of`, `Paths.get`, a typed `Path` parameter, bounded receiver aliases, `getFileName`, result aliases, and the recorded filesystem argument. Preserve the base Spring path row because lexical basename reduction does not answer link, race, platform, deployment, tenant, object-authorization, or concrete-effect questions.

**Why.** CodeQL 2.26.2 corrected `java.io.File.getName()` because the exact input `..` survives basename reduction, but the same release does not describe `Path.getFileName`. Oracle's [`Path.getFileName` contract](<https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.base/java/nio/file/Path.html#getFileName()>) defines the result as the farthest lexical name element; it does not normalize special parent names. The executable JDK witness proves `Path.of("..").getFileName().equals(Path.of(".."))` and then reads a parent `content.txt` when that result is resolved under a trusted root. The prior generic Java model nevertheless recorded any `getFileName()` or `getNameCount()` occurrence as `single-path-component-validation`. That evidence was unsound: a one-element path can be exactly `..`, so the label could bias the model toward a false negative.

**Identity and flow boundary.** Accept simple `Path.of` only with an exact `java.nio.file.Path` or wildcard import and no local `Path` declaration. Apply the same rule to `Paths.get`; fully qualified factories remain eligible despite an unrelated simple-name declaration. A typed Spring-bound `Path` parameter may be a receiver directly. A String flow must reach the exact factory argument before its returned `Path` can become a receiver. Follow only exact receiver aliases, direct factory reductions, receiver reductions, and result aliases; clear receiver and result identity on reassignment. For cross-file rows, restrict the specialization to the final wrapper parameter already proven by the Spring graph. A fixed factory beside a separately tainted sink expression, another object's `getFileName`, the wrong parameter in a multi-parameter wrapper, local lookalikes, tests, comments, strings, and cleared values fail closed.

**Control boundary.** Remove the generic single-component candidate entirely. Record `incomplete-java-nio-path-getfilename-reduction` whenever the exact reduced lineage reaches the sink. Record `parent-path-component-rejection` only when that same lineage is compared by `Path.equals` with exact official `Path.of("..")` or `Paths.get("..")` before the sink and a bounded branch returns or throws. Preserve origin lineage through candidate-path aliases so a guard on the reduced basename still applies when the sink consumes a later resolved candidate, but do not transfer a guard from a parallel reduction with a different origin. Substring checks, logging, another variable, or a post-operation check are not counterevidence. Even exact parent rejection is only a lead: provider-specific behavior, zero-element null results, absolute and volume forms, alternate separators and decoding, links, junctions, mounts, hard links, writable directories, rename races, filesystem permissions, tenant and object authorization, and the exact read/write/delete/move/create effect remain independent.

**Executable and regression evidence.** Expand the strict Spring path manifest from four to six cases. The new positive crosses controller, service, and repository boundaries before exact `Path.of(path).getFileName()` and `Files.readString`; its dependency-free witness proves the parent disclosure and an allowed `guide` read. The matched control retains identical topology and sink while comparing the same reduced `Path` with `Path.of("..")` before the operation; its witness rejects both `..` and `nested/..` and preserves `guide`. Both Maven fixtures compile against Spring Web MVC 7.0.8 with Java 17 release semantics and Maven Compiler Plugin 3.15.0; both witnesses execute on WSL Java 21. Focused regressions cover imported `Path`, imported `Paths`, fully qualified factories, typed `Path` parameters, receiver aliases, direct reductions, exact and reverse equality, origin-specific controls, weak/other/post-sink checks, local lookalikes, fixed values, reassignment, test exclusion, cross-file parameter identity, prompt guidance, and the six-case manifest. The focused lane passes 12 tests and 83 assertions.

**Acceptance evidence.** The widened Java, Spring, framework, and residual-risk lane passes 122 tests and 1,337 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,128 tests and 8,591 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional environment/integration skips and no failures. Compiled review emits one exact six-propagator specialization at the positive sink with only `incomplete-java-nio-path-getfilename-reduction`, the same exact path at the control sink with the additional `parent-path-component-rejection`, and no specialization from the production SDK's 96 deterministic records. Formatting, generated-model drift, TypeScript checking, the clean production build, and the production advisory audit pass. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; two isolated installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled-plugin files. The removed 1,484,513-byte archive had SHA-256 `97226e8447e991f8ee00b5a1f958a7df16fcef66fd90d9624355ea414e3a6da0`. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared desktop checks, and publishes a 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `0df0ed41e39780e6100a8657d4414ec2e6bcb4f0ef6fdfe2495030f5710b1f67`. WSL Ubuntu builds without warnings/errors; passes seven core, three shared, and two headless interface checks under a Linux-only PATH; and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb published-binary startup. The 72,568-byte Linux executable has SHA-256 `53c4e49e6f0aecdafec913a4d5b2423d70503f35db12c5228c0e25768fc6421d`.

**Live campaign evidence.** Authenticated campaign `0274e420f5f0008cd665d0469c5ea27d3b35e45322a61c672f17bacc832b1ee3` binds the two selected cases, fixture hashes, scanner package, runner, GitHub authentication, `gpt-5.6-terra`, high effort, deep mode, and source revision `70c96c0de259194aa738d489035bd945190b4799`. It completes both runs with one expected high-severity CWE-22 finding, one true positive, no false positives or misses, and perfect completion, precision, recall, F1, case, negative-control, stability, validation, attack-path, code-evidence, and severity metrics. The safe control completed first attempt in 4m11s with zero findings and complete coverage. The vulnerable first attempt reached the 20-minute model-turn deadline, started internal fresh session 2/3, then reached the benchmark's 25-minute scanner deadline after producing a partial draft. The runner archived that attempt and started a fresh fixture/session; attempt two completed in 17m18s with one exact controller-to-service-to-store-to-`Path.getFileName()`-to-`Files.readString` finding and complete coverage. When the host wait ended, the original runner and token-bound lock remained live; no second runner was launched, the detached owner finalized the campaign, and it removed the matching lock. Stored GitHub authentication succeeded on all sessions. No receipt or bounded diagnostic reports an allowance, credit, rate-limit, authentication, authorization, or safety-classifier failure; the only failed attempt is explicitly classified as the configured outer timeout.

**Consequence.** Copilot Security no longer treats basename-shaped `Path` output as path authorization and now supplies the reviewer with exact typed source-to-reduction-to-sink evidence instead of a generic `getFileName` hint. Future work should add repository-wide same-package Java shadow identity, static imports, helper-return summaries, non-exported method summaries, branch-sensitive dominance, custom filesystem-provider fixtures, and OS-specific handle-relative open boundaries without weakening exact factory, receiver, origin, or sink identity.

## 2026-08-05 — Serialize benchmark results with an auditable runner lock

**Decision.** Acquire one exclusive `.benchmark-runner.lock` inside the external results directory before reading or creating campaign identity, resuming attempts, invoking scanners, finalizing receipts, or replacing evaluation artifacts. The versioned JSON owner record contains only the process ID, a random 128-bit ownership token, and start time. A live owner rejects a second runner immediately. Normal release and the synchronous process-exit fallback remove the path only when its complete bytes still match the caller's token-bound record.

**Why.** The authenticated Java `File.getName()` campaign exposed a boundary that per-attempt staging could not solve. The first shell host reached its 20-minute deadline while the benchmark process tree survived and began another attempt. An identical resume command then ran concurrently in the same results directory. The scanners produced the expected one positive and zero safe findings, and evaluation passed every substantive gate, but the two runners eventually collided while promoting their private staging directories into the same canonical `run-1` paths. A shell or CI timeout is not proof that its descendants stopped. Preserving partial attempts prevents destructive retry within one runner; it does not make two independent writers safe.

**Recovery and failure boundary.** Exclusive creation is the ownership primitive. If the path exists, parse at most 16 KiB from a regular non-symbolic file and probe the recorded PID with signal zero. An alive or permission-protected PID remains active, including the conservative PID-reuse case. A dead owner's bytes must remain identical across a bounded recovery delay before the file is renamed intact under `.benchmark-runner-locks/`; acquisition then retries with a fresh token and verifies that it still owns the canonical path after competing stale inspections can settle. Malformed JSON, an unknown schema, invalid PID/token/time, oversize content, a symlink, a permission failure, or excessive ownership transitions fails closed. Recovery never overwrites canonical scan output and never treats an arbitrary pre-existing results entry as operational metadata.

**Campaign and regression evidence.** Campaign `d44245ae29499c3874b2427d72eda02de31f637301c23a21d77ed23e65ea8bdd` completed two selected runs with one expected finding, one true positive, no false positives or misses, and perfect completion, precision, recall, F1, case, negative-control, stability, validation, attack-path, code-evidence, and severity metrics. The positive required one fresh model session after a model-turn deadline and completed with substantive evidence; the safe control emitted no finding. Stored GitHub authentication succeeded throughout, with no allowance, credit, rate-limit, authentication, or classifier error. The runner's eventual nonzero status came only from the orphan/resume output collision. Focused tests now prove active-owner refusal before campaign mutation, dead-owner archival followed by campaign creation, malformed-lock retention, archive-directory lookalike rejection, token-bound release, ordinary reacquisition, SARIF seed propagation, and interrupted-run finalization; 20 tests pass with 110 assertions. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,125 tests with 19 intentional skips, no failures, and 8,562 assertions across 120 files. Formatting, generated models, types, clean build, production audit, strict 251-entry package inspection, two isolated installs, public import, CLI, and all 79 bundled files pass; the archive is removed after hashing. Windows and WSL Linux GUI build, core/shared/headless tests, publish, non-graphical startup, and X11/Xvfb startup pass.

**Consequence.** Resuming an apparently timed-out campaign can no longer spend duplicate model time or corrupt promotion merely because the original process tree outlived its host. A stale record remains auditable instead of disappearing, and unverifiable ownership requires operator inspection rather than optimistic deletion. Future work should add an explicit heartbeat or native advisory-lock backend where runtimes provide one, externally visible cancellation receipts, and process-tree termination confirmation, while retaining single-writer campaign semantics and token-bound release.

## 2026-08-05 — Keep `java.io.File.getName()` parent components tainted

**Decision.** Derive `java-file-getname-path-boundary` only from an existing `spring-http-path` record. Reopen the exact Java sink method, require official `java.io.File` identity, trace a request-derived `File` receiver or direct construction through `getName()` and bounded local aliases, and require that exact reduced result to reach the already modeled `java.nio.file` or `java.io` sink argument. Preserve the base path record because basename reduction does not answer its link, race, platform, deployment, or authorization questions.

**Why.** [CodeQL CLI 2.26.2](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-overview/codeql-changelog/codeql-cli-2.26.2/) corrected its Java partial-path-traversal model because `File.getName()` and `Path.getFileName()` are not sanitizers when the input is exactly a parent component. The JDK [`File.getName()` contract](<https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.base/java/io/File.html#getName()>) allows `new File("..").getName()` to remain `..`. Resolving that value beneath a trusted root can therefore select the root's parent even though ordinary slash-prefixed paths appear reduced to a basename. Treating every `getName` call as suspicious would recover recall at the cost of unrelated domain methods, local lookalikes, fixed metadata, tests, and values never consumed by a filesystem operation, so the scanner specializes only an already proven remote path.

**Identity and propagation boundary.** Accept simple `File` only with exact `java.io.File` or wildcard import and no repository-local shadow; always accept the fully qualified `java.io.File` spelling when the namespace itself is not replaced by the local simple type. Start from parameters of the exact exported method containing the sink, restricting a cross-file path to the wrapper parameter already proven by the base Spring graph. Preserve direct constructions only when their own constructor argument is tainted, then follow exact receiver variables, basename assignments, and bounded value aliases; clear a target when reassigned from a value outside the proven chain. Require the basename identifier in the exact sink expression. A different object's `getName`, a fixed reduction beside an unrelated remote subexpression, an unused reduction, a cleared value, a local `File` declaration, a test or plural `examples` directory, and content present only in comments or strings fail closed. The package name `example` remains eligible because it is a conventional Java package, not evidence that production code is a fixture; the executable pair caught and permanently regressed that distinction.

**Control and filesystem boundary.** Always record `incomplete-java-io-file-getname-reduction` at the reduction. Add `parent-path-component-rejection` only when the same reduced identifier is compared exactly with `..` before the sink and the bounded branch returns or throws. A substring check, logging-only branch, other variable, or post-sink check is not counterevidence. This remains a reviewer lead rather than deterministic suppression because the lightweight parser does not prove arbitrary branch dominance. Even a correct parent rejection addresses only this lexical escape; alternate separators and decoding, absolute and volume behavior, links, junctions, mounts, hard links, attacker-writable directories, rename and time-of-check/time-of-use races, filesystem permissions, tenant boundaries, object authorization, and the exact read/write/delete/move/create effect remain independent gates.

**Executable and regression evidence.** Expand the strict Spring path manifest from two to four cases. The new positive crosses controller, service, and repository boundaries, executes `new File(path).getName()`, then reads `contentRoot.resolve(basename).resolve("content.txt")`; its dependency-free witness proves `..` discloses a parent secret and an ordinary `guide` basename remains usable. The matched control retains identical topology and sink but rejects the exact reduced parent before the operation; its witness rejects both `..` and `nested/..` and still reads `guide`. Both real Spring fixtures pin Maven Compiler Plugin 3.15.0 so the Java 17 release target works on Maven 3.8.7 as well as current hosted runners.

**Acceptance evidence.** The focused Java path lane passes 9 tests and 54 assertions, including imported and fully qualified identity, receiver and direct reductions, exact rejection, weak/logging/other-variable/post-sink negatives, local shadows, reassignment, test exclusion, and the four-case manifest. The adjacent Java, Spring, framework, multi-hop, and residual slice passes 137 tests and 1,399 assertions with one intentional skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,119 tests and 8,543 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional skips and no failures. Both Spring fixtures compile on WSL Ubuntu with Java 21 and Maven 3.8.7, and both dependency-free witnesses pass with the expected exploit and rejection behavior. Compiled review emits one exact six-propagator specialization at the positive sink, the same path plus exact parent-rejection evidence for the control, and none from the 96-row SDK inventory. Formatting, generated models, types, clean build, dependency audit, strict 251-entry package inspection, two isolated installations, public import, CLI, and all 79 bundled files pass; the archive is removed after hashing. Windows and WSL Linux GUI build, core/shared/headless tests, publish, non-graphical startup, and X11/Xvfb startup pass. Prompt regressions require the reviewer to retain the exact parent-component and post-sink boundaries.

**Consequence.** Copilot Security no longer accepts basename-shaped output as proof of Java path safety and does not widen the correction into a generic `getName` heuristic. Future work should add exact `Path.getFileName()` specialization, namespace-aware multi-owner Java files, non-exported method summaries, branch-sensitive dominance, helper-based allowlist and rejection summaries, named/overloaded argument resolution, and OS-specific handle-relative open boundaries while preserving official type identity and exact source-to-result-to-sink reachability.

## 2026-08-05 — Keep `filepath.Rel` tainted until parent traversal is rejected

**Decision.** Remove `filepath.Rel` from Go filesystem candidate controls and keep its request-derived result in the existing `go-filesystem-path-construction` propagation chain. Record `relative-parent-boundary-rejection` only when one exact `Rel` assignment target is both compared with `..` and passed to exact standard-library `strings.HasPrefix` with `".." + string(os.PathSeparator)`. Attach only controls whose evidence occurs at or before the modeled sink. The candidate does not suppress inventory; the remote reviewer must still prove dominance, fail-closed behavior, and identity with the consumed path.

**Why.** [CodeQL CLI 2.26.2](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-overview/codeql-changelog/codeql-cli-2.26.2/) removed `filepath.Rel` as a sanitizer for Go path injection and Zip Slip because the result may still traverse to a parent. The host already propagated `Rel` but simultaneously described every call as a `relative-path-containment-check`. That contradictory evidence could bias the model reviewer toward a false negative. The API computes lexical relativity; it does not assert that the result is below the base, nor does it provide filesystem authorization.

**Exact boundary.** Import identity must resolve uniquely to `path/filepath`, `strings`, and `os`; lookalikes, dot or duplicate imports continue to fail closed. The derived identifier comes from the primary return of an exact `filepath.Rel` assignment. Both rejection forms must name that identifier. Equality alone misses `../child`; prefix alone misses exactly `..`; an ordinary substring or prefix check, another variable, and a check after the sink are insufficient. Structural masking proves the operations are executable code while the corresponding raw line proves the literal `..` bytes, preventing comments and string examples from supplying the missing half. Because this bounded host is not a control-flow graph, even the complete syntactic pair remains candidate evidence rather than an automatic suppression.

**Filesystem boundary.** A correct lexical parent rejection does not settle symlink, junction, mount, hard-link, attacker-writable-directory, alternate decoding, volume-relative, race, tenant, or object-authorization questions. The quality gate requires the check to dominate the exact sink and fail closed, then separately validates these filesystem and deployment properties. Root-scoped APIs under a proven fixed root remain stronger counterevidence, subject to their existing runtime patch-level requirements.

**Executable and regression evidence.** Expand the strict Go filesystem manifest from two to four cases. The new vulnerable Go 1.26 module carries an HTTP query value through one unique same-package wrapper, joins it under a public directory, computes `Rel`, rejoins the unchecked result, and reads with `os.ReadFile`; its loopback witness proves a parent payload discloses a sibling signing key. The matched control preserves request topology, payload, directories, sink, and public-file behavior while rejecting exact and separator-delimited parent results. Focused regressions prove propagation, exact control evidence, and rejection of either partial check, another variable, and post-sink validation. Hosted Go CI runs both modules on Linux.

**Live model evidence.** Campaign `b7f682152c8a8d74b64069c45467e64ae7d1300933092c12ce16120a7d1f6e08` used authenticated GitHub Copilot, `gpt-5.6-terra`, high effort, and deep mode. All four sealed cases completed: both positives matched once, both controls produced zero findings, and all twelve strict metrics passed exactly at 1 or zero false positives. A 10-minute command ceiling interrupted the first two-worker invocation after three receipts; the harness preserved those receipts, skipped them on resume, and completed only the missing control in 7 minutes 23 seconds. No allowance, authentication, rate-limit, or safety-classifier failure was observed.

**Benchmark harness correction.** Final scoring then exposed that specialized manifests still use the original `title`/`path`/`line` expectation shape and unprefixed threshold names, while the current evaluator required `id`/`locations` and `min*` names. Preserve compatibility only for an exact legacy expectation shape, derive deterministic `legacy-expectation-N` identities within each case, and canonicalize its location. Map all eleven historical rate-gate names to their current fields; reject a manifest that defines both aliases so conflicting policy cannot be hidden. A focused positive/control regression proves all twelve gates are actually enforced. This avoids rescanning an expensive sealed campaign merely because evaluation format evolved and fixes every committed specialized manifest rather than this lane alone.

**Acceptance evidence.** The focused lane passes 15 tests and 96 assertions; all eleven typed Go suites pass 299 tests and 1,773 assertions. Both modules execute and vet successfully. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,116 tests and 8,515 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional skips and no failures. Compiled inventory emits one five-propagator cross-file row at `document.go:14` with no controls for the exploit, and one structurally identical row at line 18 with the two control candidates for the safe twin. The production SDK remains 96 deterministic records and contains no Go filesystem row. Formatting, generated models, types, clean build, dependency audit, strict 251-entry package inspection, isolated public import, CLI, and all 79 bundled files pass; the archive is removed after hashing. Windows build/test/publish and WSL Ubuntu build, core/shared/headless tests, non-graphical startup, and X11/Xvfb startup all pass.

**Consequence.** The host no longer sends mutually contradictory `Rel` evidence to the model reviewer, while complete separator-aware lexical checks remain visible without becoming an unsound sanitizer. Further path-model work should add branch-sensitive dominance and named-helper summaries before strengthening this candidate into deterministic suppression; it must also preserve exact derived-value identity, error handling, separator semantics, filesystem-link and race review, import identity, and bounded analysis.

## 2026-08-05 — Treat exact Razor Page binding targets as ASP.NET remote sources

**Decision.** Extend the shared typed ASP.NET source boundary rather than adding a SQL-only signature. A public, non-static Razor Pages handler whose name begins with a supported HTTP verb after `On` contributes each remotely bindable parameter as `aspnet-razor-page-handler-parameter`. The same source feeds command execution, SQL, object authorization, template injection, SSRF, and filesystem path models through existing exact same-file, uniquely typed direct-wrapper, and one-relay sink machinery. Follow an official `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.RazorPages.PageModel` base directly or through at most eight uniquely resolved local class edges; preserve the exact handler declaration as source evidence.

**Why.** [CodeQL CLI 2.26.0](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-overview/codeql-changelog/codeql-cli-2.26.0/) added Razor Page handler parameters as C# remote-flow sources. Its exact framework library identifies `PageModel` inheritance and `On%` handlers, while [ASP.NET Core model-binding documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/mvc/models/model-binding) confirms that Razor Pages handler parameters are binding targets without controller-style `[FromQuery]` annotations. The scanner previously required an ASP.NET source attribute or `Request.*`, erasing complete handler-to-service-to-sink paths. A raw `OnGet` name match would restore recall at the cost of flagging ordinary methods and local lookalikes, so framework identity, handler accessibility, value reachability, and opt-outs remain mandatory.

**Beyond-parity property boundary.** Also model official `[BindProperty]` and class-level `[BindProperties]` public writable `PageModel` properties as `aspnet-razor-page-bound-property`. Non-GET handlers bind these opted-in properties by default; GET and HEAD handlers require the exact attribute surface to set `SupportsGet = true`. Follow the property or one bounded local alias into the consumed sink/call expression. A static property, non-public setter, official `[BindNever]`, local/parameter shadow, property or member reassignment before the sink, local attribute lookalike, or unbound property rejects the path. This captures a documented Razor Pages input surface not covered by the upstream 2.26.0 handler-parameter note while keeping the binding decision auditable.

**Identity and failure boundary.** Unqualified `PageModel` requires the official Razor Pages using and no repository-local `PageModel` declaration; fully qualified and `global::` bases are accepted only when the official namespace is not shadowed. Local inheritance must resolve one simple base type to one `.cs` file at every edge. Duplicate names, qualified unknown intermediates, external bases, cycles, and a ninth edge fail closed. Only public non-static HTTP-verb handlers participate. Official `[NonHandler]` excludes the exact method. `[FromServices]`, `[FromKeyedServices]`, `[BindNever]`, `CancellationToken`, provider/logging interfaces, page/request/response contexts, principals, and other infrastructure-shaped parameters are not remote user values. Comments and ordinary strings are structurally masked before every identity and call decision.

**Flow and compact-call correction.** Synthetic Razor sources only activate a model; they never use the old nearest-source fallback. The host reparses the containing method, calculates which exact parameters or property-derived aliases reach the sink expression or wrapper argument, and clears flow on reassignment. Existing controller annotations retain their compatibility fallback for top-level snippets that have no parseable owner. While exercising all six sink families, one-line handler bodies exposed that the shared C#/Java call slicer selected the method signature's first parenthesis. It now skips a signature call only when the structural line begins with an access-controlled method declaration and a body brace follows it. This condition avoids treating later SQL object-initializer braces as method bodies—a regression that would otherwise reconnect a safe `SqlParameter` value to fixed query text.

**Executable and regression evidence.** `aspnet-razor-page-sql-manifest.json` pairs two real .NET 8 Razor Pages applications under perfect gates. Both expose named `OnGetLookupAsync(string filter)` and cross the exact `_queries:UserQueries` receiver, `LookupAsync[0]` argument, and wrapper parameter. The positive concatenates `filter` into `SqlCommand` argument zero; its hermetic SQL implementation proves `"' OR '1'='1"` selects `Administrator`. The control retains the same remotely bound bytes and topology but uses fixed SQL plus a typed `SqlParameter`; its witness rejects the payload and returns `Alice` for the exact allowed value. Focused tests cover all six ASP.NET sinks, direct and multi-hop wrappers, named/async handlers, fully qualified `PageModel`, bounded local inheritance, POST property binding, GET `SupportsGet`, class-level binding, local alias flow, service and non-handler exclusions, reassignment, local type/attribute shadows, and the paired fixture.

**Acceptance evidence.** The focused lane passes 7 tests and 28 assertions; the adjacent ASP.NET/framework slice passes 50 tests and 237 assertions. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,112 tests and 8,486 assertions across 120 files with 19 intentional skips and no failures. Both .NET fixtures build without warnings or errors and both witnesses pass. Compiled review emits one `aspnet-http-sql` cross-file record at `Services/UserQueries.cs:19` for the positive, with source `Pages/Search.cshtml.cs:17` and the three exact typed propagators; the matched control emits none. Production SDK review remains 96 rows and emits no Razor Pages source. Formatting, generated models, types, clean build, dependency audit, and strict 251-entry package inspection pass; isolated installation validates public import, CLI, and all 79 bundled files. Windows GUI build/test/publish and WSL Ubuntu build, shared/core/headless tests, non-graphical startup, and X11/Xvfb startup all pass. The WSL core gate deliberately uses a Linux-only PATH after an initial diagnostic run proved that inherited Windows `node.exe` is not executable as a Linux process.

**Consequence.** Copilot Security now covers Razor Pages as a first-class remote-input framework across the scanner's complete typed ASP.NET sink pack, and it covers explicit bound properties beyond the upstream handler-parameter delta. Future work should add exact custom `IModelBinder` and `BindAsync` semantics, route-convention reachability, namespace-aware multi-owner files, named C# argument mapping, record/primary-constructor page models, deeper but still auditable property-member propagation, and framework-version-aware binding changes. Each extension must preserve exact official identity, binding role, HTTP method, opt-out, reassignment, receiver, argument, control, and sink semantics rather than broadening through names alone.

## 2026-08-05 — Specialize SSRF paths with incomplete IPv6-transition guards

**Decision.** Add `node-ssrf-ipv6-transition-incomplete-guard` as a derived typed model rather than a validator-name alert. The host first requires an existing `node-http-ssrf` source-to-sink proof. It then reparses the exact native HTTP URL argument, resolves the exported sink wrapper, derives parsed-host aliases from the consumed URL, and requires a fail-closed branch using an explicitly IPv4-only classifier or dotted-quad private-range literals on that same host before the sink. The derived record preserves the original scope, source, sink, and ordered propagators, identifies the guard line as `incomplete-ipv6-transition-address-guard`, and records CWE-918 plus CWE-1389.

**Why.** CodeQL 2.26.0 introduced an experimental [JavaScript IPv6-transition SSRF guard query](https://github.com/github/codeql/blob/main/javascript/ql/src/experimental/Security/CWE-918/SsrfIpv6TransitionIncompleteGuard.ql). Its heuristic usefully exposes a gap in dotted-quad filtering, but function naming or a known package alone does not prove attacker flow, sink reachability, same-value validation, or dominance. Its safe examples also accept explicit mapped-address handling without requiring NAT64 and 6to4 closure. Building on the scanner's exact SSRF graph retains the useful new class while raising both the exploit and counterevidence standards.

**Transition and suppression boundary.** Treat IPv4-mapped IPv6 (`::ffff:`), well-known-prefix NAT64 (`64:ff9b::`), and 6to4 (`2002:`) independently. One-prefix normalization is incomplete. Suppress the specialization only when the sink wrapper assigns a transition-named canonicalizer result, the exact result reaches the fail-closed private-address condition, and that canonicalizer's own bounded function body contains all three families plus embedded-address extraction. Comments are removed before this proof. A complete but unused helper, a canonicalized value not consumed by the guard, a guard on another request value, a log-only branch, URL parsing by itself, and generic address-validation words remain insufficient. Conventional `.test`/`.spec`, `test`/`tests`/`tests-ts`/`__tests__`, and example paths are excluded to avoid promoting embedded fixture strings or deliberately unsafe tests into product findings. This is still a deterministic hypothesis: final validation must prove the deployed stack accepts and routes the exact literal, then close DNS, proxy, redirect, connection-pinning, Host/TLS, metadata-range, and internal-impact questions.

**Regression and benchmark evidence.** `node-ipv6-transition-ssrf-manifest.json` pairs a relative-module exploit with the same topology and a complete three-family canonicalizer under perfect completion, precision, recall, validation, attack-path, code-evidence, severity, and negative-case gates. The positive record retains source `src/server.js:4`, wrapper declaration `src/upstream.js:5`, broken guard `src/upstream.js:7`, sink `src/upstream.js:8`, and the ordered import/call/parameter propagators. Deterministic negatives cover complete canonicalization, an unused complete helper, a mapped-only helper, unrelated-host checks, non-fail-closed logging, absent guards, and comment-only transition text. The standalone witness uses Node's `net.isIP` to prove `::ffff:127.0.0.1`, `64:ff9b::7f00:1`, and `2002:7f00:1::` are accepted IPv6 literals, bypass the dotted-quad predicate, and canonicalize to blocked loopback.

**Acceptance evidence.** The focused lane passes 9 tests and 24 assertions. The adjacent SSRF, Axios, framework, and residual suite passes 99 tests and 1,204 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,105 tests with 19 intentional environment/integration skips, no failures, and 8,458 assertions across 119 files. Both witness tests pass. Compiled deterministic review emits one specialized whole-repository row at the intentional vulnerable fixture, none for the safe twin, and none from the SDK source tree after conventional test/example exclusion. Formatting, generated models, TypeScript checking, the clean build, and the production dependency audit pass. Strict package inspection validates 251 entries; two isolated installations validate public import, CLI behavior, and all 79 bundled-plugin files. Windows builds without warnings/errors, passes seven core and three shared desktop checks, and publishes a fresh 346,796-byte executable with SHA-256 `F5037A6D8DAA4B14E32D45BCD07B47B57C3567B841323DDF5EB055613C322F85`. WSL Ubuntu builds without warnings/errors; passes seven core, three shared desktop, and two headless interface checks; and passes non-graphical plus X11/Xvfb published-binary startup. The final scanner archive was 1,460,269 bytes with SHA-1 `d927ef3b85add2ba8097e12cfdd86971634eb43e`, SHA-256 `4d3e9bc43550cba9b928445e8eb988c5774e380a779aa8f8655359a69ffd6584`, and integrity `sha512-WRE8d3RGLGyIzDo8XFuqo2qFWODpT3H97+6cOBdyk7DwzyaWFAsibAwbgG9bt8dA7bGG/rU309DI4A7EkSx6JQ==` before removal.

**Consequence.** Copilot Security can now distinguish “there is address validation near an HTTP request” from “that validation is incomplete for alternate routable representations” without discarding the original attack path. Future extensions should bind exact third-party IP-library identities and semantics, parse additional transition/tunnel forms only when the target stack supports them, model non-exported sink closures without widening lexical ambiguity, and integrate DNS-answer and connection-address identity into the same typed path.

## 2026-08-05 — Model GitHub Copilot SDK trusted-instruction injection

**Decision.** Add a typed `node-copilot-system-prompt-injection` lane for CWE-1427. Activation requires an exact named `CopilotClient` import or CommonJS destructuring from `@github/copilot-sdk`, one uniquely constructed non-reassigned client, and that receiver's `createSession` or `resumeSession` call. From that exact configuration, retain only `systemMessage.content`, consumed customize-section content, known-section transform callbacks, inference-visible `customAgents[].prompt` and `.description`, and `tools[].description`. Preserve the specific trusted field as the sink rather than labeling the session call generically.

**Why.** CodeQL 2.26.0 added a high-precision JavaScript [system prompt injection query](https://codeql.github.com/codeql-query-help/javascript/js-system-prompt-injection/) for untrusted data crossing into higher-priority model instructions. The official [GitHub Copilot SDK session configuration](https://github.com/github/copilot-sdk/blob/main/nodejs/src/types.ts) exposes that boundary in several forms rather than one `systemPrompt` parameter: an append/replace/customize `systemMessage`, custom agents, and model-visible tool metadata. Its current command description is completion-UI metadata and is therefore deliberately excluded. The SDK's customization behavior also accepts content under an unknown section name by appending it as additional instructions. Modeling only the obvious top-level field would miss reachable trusted text, while treating every session prompt as system text would incorrectly flag the intended user-message channel.

**Trust and control boundary.** Append mode retains the runtime foundation but does not demote appended attacker text; replace mode also removes managed guardrails. Fixed content selected through an exact server-owned allowlist is retained as candidate counterevidence only when unknown keys fail closed and the selected value—not the request key—reaches the sink. String escaping, JSON encoding, XML-like delimiters, warnings, and instructions to ignore embedded text do not change hierarchy. In contrast, a fixed trusted configuration followed by request data only in `session.send` or `sendAndWait` is a strong negative control. An unknown customize section with content is still a sink because of fallback append semantics; a content-free unknown `remove` or `preserve` entry is not.

**Bounded identity and flow.** Parse complete object and array entries without evaluating source. Resolve named configurations, system-message objects, array elements, section actions, agent-inference flags, and value aliases through at most eight declarations in a 64-line bounded region. The shared initializer slicer anchors after the actual assignment operator and skips whitespace from original source, rather than letting masked quoted content move the right-hand-side boundary. Same-file request flow records the exact request source and trusted property. Existing provider-neutral JavaScript summaries preserve that property through either one direct relative-module wrapper or one relay plus a sink wrapper, recording all ordered import, call-argument, and parameter edges. A wrong package, default or namespace guess, duplicate binding, client parameter, client reassignment, unrelated same-named method, fixed field, ninth alias, comment, or string example rejects the proof. Direct chained construction and unresolved external wrappers remain deliberately unmodeled rather than guessed.

**Regression and benchmark evidence.** `node-copilot-prompt-injection-manifest.json` adds a paired three-file exploit and control under perfect completion, precision, recall, validation, attack-path, evidence, severity, and negative-case gates. The exploit carries an HTTP persona through six ordered propagators into `systemMessage.content`; the control preserves the modules and fixed system message but sends request values only as an ordinary prompt. Focused tests cover eight trusted surfaces, `resumeSession`, ESM aliases, CommonJS destructuring, unknown-section fallback, exact acceptance at eight value aliases, fixed allowlist evidence, and adversarial failures for the ninth alias, lookalike/default/namespace imports, reassignment, ignored `remove`/`preserve` content, non-inferred agent descriptions, completion-UI command metadata, unrelated clients, inert properties, comments, and string pseudo-flows. The focused lane passes 7 tests and 36 assertions.

**Acceptance evidence.** The adjacent Node/framework/residual gate passes 114 tests and 1,340 assertions with one intentional platform skip. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,096 tests with 19 intentional skips, no failures, and 8,434 assertions across 118 files. Compiled-output review emits exactly one CWE-1427 hypothesis for the exploit: source `src/server.js:4`, sink `src/session.js:9`, and all six ordered relative-import, call-argument, and wrapper-parameter propagators. The user-message control emits none. Deterministic production-SDK review emits 96 residual rows and no prompt-injection hypothesis; no authenticated remote self-scan or repository-history export is used. Windows builds without warnings or errors, passes seven core plus three shared desktop checks, and publishes a 346,796-byte single-file executable with SHA-256 `96700B3A889143EA8B2B3B657D48C6A133CF863A287CAE01A0751A181E98476D`. WSL Ubuntu builds without warnings or errors, passes seven core, three shared desktop, and two headless Avalonia checks, and starts the published binary in both non-graphical and X11/Xvfb modes. Formatting, generated models, TypeScript checking, the clean build, dependency audit, strict package inspection, two isolated installs, public import, CLI, and all 79 bundled-plugin checks are clean. The validated 251-entry archive was 1,437,137 bytes packed and 6,683,548 bytes unpacked, had SHA-1 `528fb8ada759b0e6c870490cd4ed828c642acce8`, integrity `sha512-lkH709OgXPOCEXm+STsNqx3r6bDbKt+TP/YEARSpcqygKFm0zWQeh6eebxqMIq2/38c6zDK02UUq3VKBVCVnng==`, and was removed.

**Consequence.** The residual pass can now surface a modern AI-application trust-boundary defect with typed source-to-sink evidence instead of relying on generic words such as `prompt` or `agent`. Reviewer guidance separately proves exposure and impact across tools, commands, MCP servers, permissions, filesystem/network reachability, credentials, tenant isolation, disclosure, and the concrete unintended operation. Remaining work includes exact direct-construction receivers, bounded callback/request-object property flow, more module systems and build-resolved aliases, indirect agent/tool registration APIs, model-provider-independent trusted-message interfaces, and deeper wrapper graphs without sacrificing binding identity or the ordinary-user-message negative boundary.

## 2026-08-05 — Resolve promoted Go methods through embedded concrete structs

**Decision.** Extend the object-authorization receiver graph with exact local concrete method promotion. Parse an embedded field only in one of Go's supported named forms, derive its unqualified field name, and retain whether it embeds `T` or `*T`. Resolve a selector breadth-first from the outer concrete struct. At each depth, count both fields and methods with the requested name: a direct declaration hides deeper names, and a shallow depth is usable only when it contains exactly one method and no competing field. Follow at most eight embedded fields, preserve exact directory, package, import, and exported visibility identity, and record every traversed field as `go-method-receiver-promoted-field` evidence.

**Why.** Go services commonly embed a repository or a repository-bearing layer and call its promoted methods as if they belonged to the service. The previous parser accepted only explicitly named fields, so `service.DeleteInvoice(...)` disappeared even when the complete handler, constructor, local struct graph, method declaration, object flow, and database effect were present. Resolving by outer type and method name would be unsafe because Go selectors choose the unique name at the shallowest depth and because `T`, `*T`, an embedded `T`, and an embedded `*T` expose different method sets. The bounded breadth-first graph reproduces those language rules without inventing dynamic dispatch.

**Selector, method-set, and failure boundary.** Interface satisfaction begins with the actual method set: a value `T` admits value-receiver methods, while `*T` admits value and pointer receivers. Traversing embedded `T` preserves that pointer capability; traversing embedded `*T` makes both receiver forms available to either outer method set. An ordinary concrete variable call is separately allowed to use pointer methods because the modeled binding is addressable. A direct field or method wins before any deeper traversal. Same-depth collisions are ambiguous even when they eventually reach the same receiver type. A cycle, ninth edge, unresolved or external embedded type, embedded interface, generic or unsupported declaration, inaccessible unexported method, duplicate method, missing local struct identity, or pointer/value mismatch rejects resolution. No external package or compiler approximation is inferred.

**Receiver-state correction.** A promoted pointer method exists in the static method set even when the embedded pointer is nil, but such a call cannot reach the recorded database effect. Each embedded pointer edge therefore creates an exact receiver requirement on the constructor/binding graph. The new nil-pointer controls exposed a terminal composition defect: wrapper-edge construction carried call-site requirements, but direct matching of the final callee enforced only requirements already stored inside the callee summary. Direct matching now combines the target call's requirements with every callee requirement prefixed by the promoted receiver path before accepting the binding. This fix applies uniformly to promoted, explicit concrete-field, and narrowed interface-field dispatch.

**Executable and regression evidence.** Expand the strict Go authorization manifest from sixty-eight to seventy cases with paired Go 1.26 modules. `Service` embeds `RepositoryLayer`, which embeds `*store.Store`; an exact constructor initializes the pointer before the handler invokes the promoted deletion. The exploit's ID-only query changes witness state for an attacker-selected victim. The control retains both embeddings and the constructor but adds the context-derived account predicate; its witness rejects the victim and deletes an owned invoice. Focused positives cover nested cross-package concrete calls, value and pointer interface satisfaction, addressable value calls, and shallow direct-method precedence. Negatives cover uninitialized embedded pointers under both interface and concrete calls, same-depth method ambiguity, a shallower field collision, cyclic pointer embeddings, an unresolved external embedding, a value that lacks the required pointer method set, and exact acceptance at eight promotion edges with rejection at nine.

**Acceptance evidence.** The focused lane passes 133 tests and 881 assertions. All eleven typed Go suites pass 296 tests and 1,749 assertions, and all seventy manifest-backed Go authorization modules execute successfully. Both new modules pass execution and vet. The authoritative Windows suite passes 1,089 tests with 19 intentional skips, no failures, and 8,398 assertions across 117 files. ## 2026-08-05 — Follow bounded local interface aliases into Go type switches

**Decision.** Resolve a type-switch source through zero to eight exact local value-flow edges rather than requiring the source identifier itself to be a parameter. Seed the environment with every uniquely named function parameter whose non-pointer interface identity passes the existing module/import/type resolver. Rejecting it loses a provable all-path initialization even though the eventual field writes still use the exact original parameter and every case converges. Current [CodeQL Go data-flow guidance](https://codeql.github.com/docs/codeql-language-guides/analyzing-data-flow-in-go/) defines recursive `localFlow` over zero or more local value-flow steps, while [Semgrep's glossary](https://semgrep.dev/docs/writing-rules/glossary) describes explicit propagators for flow a rule must carry. Matching that useful alias capability is necessary for recall; retaining an explicit depth cap, lexical depth, exact interface seed, kill-on-write semantics, and executable paired controls makes this host's proof boundary narrower and more auditable than generic taint reachability.

**Value, scope, and failure boundary.** A direct parameter is depth zero. `alias1 := repository` through `alias8 := alias7` are admitted; an alias derived from depth eight is not. `var alias = repository` and exact ordinary rebindings use the same value-preserving rule. Rebinding from another seeded interface parameter is valid and changes the source identity exactly. Every assignment kills its target before possible reintroduction, so a concrete composite, call, conversion, selector, literal, arithmetic or other expression clears provenance. Multiple left-side names and tuple values do not propagate. An assignment observed at nested structural depth clears matching names conservatively and never creates an outer alias, preventing branch-local or shadow-ambiguous flow from escaping. ## 2026-08-05 — Replay exact interface type switches without changing receiver provenance

**Decision.** Extend bounded constructor and constructor-helper switch recognition to two type-switch grammars: `switch source.(type) {` and `switch target := source.(type) {`. The source interface's dynamic value chooses a case but does not change receiver provenance when each arm writes the original parameter and all resulting graphs converge. A named Go type-switch guard must be used for the program to compile; a blank assignment is the narrowest compiler-valid use and has no value or state effect. Admitting arbitrary guard use would let a narrowed dynamic value, method call, or assignment change the implementation being tracked. Resolving the source as one exact interface parameter and recognizing only a leading blank assignment closes the real false negative without claiming general type narrowing or data-flow semantics.

Built-in `any` is deliberately not treated as an unconditional alias because a package may shadow it. The target cannot be `_`, a parameter, receiver, package alias, prior local, or prior local type. Zero or more blank lines and exact `_ = target` statements may precede an arm's writes; a no-op after a substantive statement, or any target occurrence in a field value, call, expression, declaration, or other statement, rejects the switch. ## 2026-08-04 — Preserve exact state through nested Go constructor writes

**Decision.** Replace the constructor summary's flat field-expression map with a recursive value-state tree. Preserve the original expression, keyed-composite type, child map, source line, and creation-versus-write origin for every node. Permit an exact constructor-local assignment to traverse materialized parent composites and replace only the selected leaf. Carry that tree into call-site type and interface resolution so evidence records the parent initialization and later nested write independently.

**Why.** Constructors often allocate a layer or configuration object before injecting its repository, client, or policy dependency. Flattening the outer field cannot represent a later `service.layer.repository = repository` without either missing the path or inventing the parent. A recursive state tree proves that each parent exists and retains the implementation selected at the leaf. Per-node origins also avoid falsely attributing the later injection to the earlier parent composite.

**Copy and alias boundary.** Pointer result aliases share the complete constructor state. Copying a value result copies each concrete value field recursively but shares pointer fields, matching Go's shallow assignment semantics. A returned summary receives an independent deep snapshot so later source text cannot mutate the result. Interface or otherwise dynamic parent state is not traversed without a materialized keyed composite. Direct top-level writes retain their existing copied-value behavior.

**Write, type, and resource boundary.** A nested write must be top-level and linear, use a direct selector or explicit pointer dereference, traverse at most eight fields, and find a keyed-composite state at every parent. The leaf reuses exact local-module, import, struct, pointer/value, interface, full method-set, parameter, and concrete implementation validation. Reassignment checks use each leaf's own line. The existing eight-write and thirteen-line statement budgets remain. Missing parents, wrong fields, invalid dereferences, conditional or nested-block writes, a ninth path field, a ninth write, unresolved dynamic state, and ambiguity fail closed. Overwriting a repository leaf discards the stale implementation and selects the replacement exactly.

## 2026-08-04 — Preserve Go receiver identity through nested constructor composites

**Decision.** Recursively materialize receiver-bearing fields in keyed Go composite literals. When an exact constructor field contains another exact repository-local struct composite, retain its nested concrete or interface field map rather than reducing the expression to only the outer type. Ignore unrelated scalar initialization, because it does not change receiver identity, while preserving a separate `go-method-receiver-composite-field` evidence item for every tracked nested assignment.

**Why.** Production constructors often create a service graph inline: a service owns a pointer to a layer, and that layer owns the injected repository interface. The previous one-level summary proved the outer pointer type but discarded the repository instance inside it, causing an avoidable false negative. Treating every keyed value as receiver state would instead make labels, timeouts, and other scalars spuriously determine dispatch. Recursive typed field maps preserve the relevant heap-construction fact without pretending to model arbitrary state.

**Parsing and resource boundary.** Parse only keyed composites with one exact simple local or authoritative imported struct type. Accept the trailing comma required by ordinary multiline Go formatting. A constructor assignment or return may span at most thirteen masked structural lines and must balance parentheses, brackets, and braces; a fourteenth line fails closed. Recursion shares the existing eight-field selector limit and constructor-depth budget. Positional, unbalanced, missing, duplicate, unresolved, or over-depth fields are rejected. Lines that are not an assignment or return never enter the multiline join, so conditions and nested blocks retain their existing reassignment and nesting semantics.

**Type, interface, and parameter boundary.** Track a field only when its declaration resolves to one concrete repository-local struct or one local basic interface. Concrete fields require exact directory, package, type, and pointer identity. Interface fields require one unique method-set-compatible implementation for every directly declared method; a value cannot satisfy a pointer-only method. Constructor parameters used anywhere in the nested expression are mapped to exact call-site bindings and must not be reassigned before construction. Primitive scalar fields are deliberately skipped. Missing bindings, wrong inner types, unbound interfaces, incompatible method sets, duplicate receiver methods, and unresolved implementation alternatives fail closed.

The positive witness proves deletion through only the selected primary sink. The control preserves the entire graph and adds only principal scope.
